#3005. Brad Stevens talks Mitchell Robinson, Celtics’ start, and more

SACRAMENTO — Brad Stevens took a lot of heat this offseason for pulling the trigger on the trade that sent Jaylen Brown to the Philadelphia 76ers and ended a near-decade-long partnership with Jayson Tatum.

And, though it's too soon to tell, the Celtics are 11-4, have the second-best net rating in the NBA, the second-best defense, and the sixth-best offense. Paul George, the team's biggest offseason acqusition, has been generally awesome. The team's significant depth has been on full display regularly.

Still, Stevens said it's too soon to judge who this team really is, and that he normally waits until mid-December to make those kinds of evaluations.

"I've been encouraged by a lot of things," Stevens said. "I think we've still got a lot to figure out to be the best version of ourselves. But usually, I'm probably another couple of weeks or a month away from getting too deep into, okay, this is who we really are. Yet, I think we have played some good basketball. We've shown ourselves capable of it. We've gotten good play from a lot of different guys, and the team has really tried to buy in, I think, well, to what the coaches are asking them to do on both sides of the ball."

Paul George has been one of the brightest spots of the Celtics' early-season success. George has been tremendous defensively, and he's also averaging 19.9 points, 5.5 rebounds, and 3.7 assists per game, while shooting 46.5% from the field and 42.3% from three. He's had two games with at least 35 points and has been one of the team's most reliable defenders.

"Paul's been a good fit," Stevens said. "Paul's a really good player…. You don't come with those accolades if you're not able to impact the game. Everybody talks about what he can do on offense, and I think the minute he stepped on the court with our guys defensively, our guys, a lot of them were kind of taken aback, even though playing against him all these years, with how good he is, how active he is, how smart he is, how aware he is, and he's just really had a good impact on the group. He's just been a joy to be around. He's been great."

The Celtics' other marquee offseason acquisition, Mitchell Robinson, hasn't transitioned as seamlessly as George. Robinson has the worst on-off rating on the Celtics at -30.1 (that number measures the difference in net rating when a player is on the floor versus off of it). The historically prolific rebounder is also averaging the fewest rebounds per game (5.4) of his career.

"You can tell it's obviously a transition for him, and that's not all that atypical for guys, and so again, it's so early that we'll see how this all plays out," Stevens said. "But I trust that what he does when he's at his best will translate because it always has, right? And I think that getting him to the rim on both sides of the floor, getting him to be in that area where he can be most impactful, [is] something that we'll continue to work on — continue to put him in position where, hopefully, he can soar with his strengths."

Then, there's rookie Chris Cenac Jr., who began the year with the Maine Celtics but has found himself in the Celtics' rotation over the past two games. Cenac, who was drafted 27th overall, tallied 6 points and 6 rebounds in 17 minutes in the Celtics' loss to the Nuggets on Wednesday night.

"He's been solid," Stevens said. "I'm really encouraged. I really like him. I think he's got a great work ethic. He's obviously got physical tools. He's got a good mind for the game. He's gonna be good, right? I do think that with each step, there are different expectations. So he's in that honeymoon phase of, well, he did one good thing and it looks awesome, and wow. And then your expectations grow and grow, and it's just like we've seen with Jordan and Hugo and Baylor. Once you taste it, once you get a little feeling, then the expectation is to be even better, and we see that all the way up and down our roster. All of our guys, for the most part, have been through that. But I'm encouraged by the start. It's hard not to be, and I think the other part of it is he does have the physical ability to continue to raise his level in big moments."

One of the other interesting aspects of the roster is the logjam at the wing position; Sam Hauser (20.3 minutes), Baylor Scheierman (19.4 minutes), Jordan Walsh (15.7 minutes), and Hugo Gonzalez (12.9 minutes) have all seen significant minutes, but no one player in that group has pulled away to play the majority of the minutes.

Each brings something different to the table; Hauser (46.8% from three) and Scheierman (42.2% from three) have been the biggest threats from beyond the arc, while Gonzalez (7.1 rebounds/36 minutes) has been the best rebounder of the bunch. From the wings, Walsh has probably been the team's best on-ball defender and its most consistent starter, with 10 starts in 15 games.

But the players have seemed interchangeable at times, and no one has clearly emerged as the best player in the pack.

"I said at the start of the year, the biggest challenge for the coaching staff is the wing differentiation," Stevens said. "That's been the hardest part, and I think they've managed it beautifully, because nobody has really differentiated themselves, and they're all good players. And so that's just kind of the hard part about having depth, which in this league you need now. It's really important, but it also comes with the challenges of — not everybody's going to get exactly what they want on every given night. Is it easier to have seven or eight guys that you know are going to play every night? Yeah, until two of them get hurt, then you're in trouble, right? And so that's part of the challenge in this day and age with how the league is going and how hard it is to play the full 82, and certainly game to game with what's required on both ends."

As the team continues to improve on both ends, Stevens is generally encouraged by what he's seen.

"One of the things that this group really has is a lot of feel for how to play, and you see that when we're at our very best on both ends: the reaction, the reads, the ability to play off one another, the ability to cover for one another on the defensive end," Stevens said. "I think that those are things that, as the season goes on, we should continue to benefit from, to be able to grow, kind of however Joe and the staff want us to play throughout that given stretch, and ultimately to be the best that we can be."

Michael Penix Jr. has struggled to take control of the offense, throwing seven interceptions to go along with his 10 touchdowns. In Week 8, he threw four picks in a loss to the Tampa Bay Buccaneers.

The NFC South is one of the weaker divisions in the league and the Falcons aren't totally out of the playoff picture. But Week 11 is a chance for them to recalibrate to avoid missing the playoffs for the ninth straight year.

#2998. Sixers get lucky with their 14th win as Herro can’t get off game-tying three in time

The Sixers just outlasted the Milwaukee Bucks by a literal second, hanging on to win 132-129 Saturday afternoon, notching their fifth win in a row and improving to 14-3 on the season.

Joel Embiid did a lot to make up for a subpar shooting night, finishing with a team-high 26 points, a season-high 17 rebounds, and nine assists shooting 7-of-17 from the floor. After missing last game with a foot sprain, Tyrese Maxey had one of his quietest games of the year finishing with 17 points on 4-of-11 shooting.

LeBron James played quarterback for much of the day, finishing with 21 points, six rebounds and six assists going 9-of-14. VJ Edgecombe shot 5-of-12 from the floor to finish with 16 points with three rebounds and six assists. Tyler Herro led all scorers with 50 but his three to tie the game just didn't beat the buzzer.

Jaylen Brown and Dean Wade missed their second straight game, both dealing with hamstring strains, along with Adem Bona, who's missed the last eight. Milwaukee was without Ousmane Dieng.

The Sixers got two of their easiest buckets all season to open the game off of cuts. James went down the baseline and Edgecombe flashed to the middle of the paint. Their pull-up jump shooting was a different story early. Embiid even had a midrange blocked by Myles Turner as the Sixers struggled to capitalize on a 1-of-8 start for the Bucks.

Embiid also had a layup attempt blocked by Turner as he started the game 0-of-4 from the field. His other two jumpers falling just off the rim. On the other end the Sixers allowed two offensive rebounds to turn into second-chance points as the Bucks' offense was the first to find a rhythm.

The Sixers' offense settled in after an early timeout from Nick Nurse. They got a couple of open threes from Anfernee Simons and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, Edgecombe broke Turner down off the dribble, and LeBron opened up plenty of space on a drive to hit Embiid with a lob. James made a great one-handed pass cross court to find KCP in the corner a few possessions later but the three rimmed out. KCP and Embiid also both missed fast break layups that halted a run.

Jabari Walker checked in next to Embiid at the four like he has in recent games and was rewarded for cutting by Embiid shortly after checking in. Dominick Barlow made his season debut a few minutes later, relieving Embiid. Labaron Philon Jr. remained in the rotation and picked up a floater, but also fouled Herro attempting a three right before the quarter ended. Herro made all three to give the Bucks a one-point lead after one.

Barlow's first big impact was tipping back a missed Maxey floater for Philly's first points of the second. On the next possession he ran down the floor to catch a lob after LeBron had just about singlehandedly created. It wasn't all perfect — the Bucks scored six straight field goals in the paint, but it was a fine first shift back for Barlow.

LeBron was easily the best player on the floor for the Sixers. His ability to lead people open gave them a lot of easy baskets that were much needed for an offense struggling to hit jumpers. They were also struggling to finish off defensive possessions as the Bucks picked up their 10th second-chance point on eight offensive rebounds.

Maxey got downhill quick and drew a foul which gave Nurse a chance to use a timeout. Coming out of that break, it seemed like there was an ever bigger focus to run quickly, whether if it was after a make or a miss. After Maxey zoomed through for a layup, Edgecombe got down the floor twice, setting up Walker and Embiid for buckets at the rim.

It was weird because the Sixers weren't playing great defense by any means, but the Bucks still had a bunch of airballs in the first half, more than any NBA team should. The misses the Sixers got were ugly, but they hadn't made life hard on Herro at all — he was able to get to just about every spot on the floor.

The first trouble Herro ran into all night was when he tried to contest an Embiid dunk in transition and took a shot to the face while unsuccessfully doing so. The dunk seemed to finally get Embiid going as he was able to bully past Turner for an and-1 after trying all half to do so. That capped off an 8-0 run that gave the Sixers a one-point lead at the break.

Sandwiched in between a pair of free throws from Maxey and Embiid, LeBron got to the basket for another big dunk. A few possessoins later, Embiid finally got a midrange to go over Turner. Not much had changed defensively as the Bucks scored on their first four possessions of the second half.

The Sixers definitley got more comfortable in their half court offense as the game went on. After paint buckets from Maxey and Edgecombe, KCP knocked a three to give the Sixers a lead they were able to hang on to for more than one possession. Aside from losing Herro once on the perimeter as they came down the floor, the defense had improved as well. Embiid had started to do a good job of finishing possessions as he passed his season high in rebounds.

Simons and KCP were the only Sixers who had made a three to this point in the game, so it was a good thing they were both feeling it. Simons ripped eight straight points to give the Sixers their first double-digit lead of the night.

Embiid threw a nice one-handed pass into the corner for Edgecombe who finally became the third Sixer of the night to hit a three. Edgecombe tried to run quickly off a made free throw, but had his layup attempt blocked by Kel'el Ware. Simons got a chance for yet another buzzer-beater, but his midrange attempt didn't fall, neither did Embiid's humorous attempt to get a shot off with 0.2 seconds remaining. The Sixers held a six-point lead after the third.

Barlow did get up a corner three in his first shift and airballed it pretty bad. He got another chance in his second shift and got plenty of arc to bury it this time. LeBron found him from the high-post for a layup on the next possession to prompt an early Bucks timeout.

Shortly after James found Barlow cutting to the basket again and hit him for the layup, drawing a foul in the process. The Sixers lost their grip on Herro coming out of the timeout though, letting him get to the basket a couple of times. The Sixers nearly forced a turnover, but left AJ Green open in the process, who was able to hit a three to cut it back to a one-possession game.

After calling a timeout, the Sixers put the ball in LeBron's hands, who was able to back in his way in for a bucket, putting back his own missed floater. Thanks to another offensive rebound from Barlow on the next possession, Maxey was able to scoot to the basket for two more. James bullied his way for two more baskets, drawing a foul on one, to restore order a bit.

Milwaukee was able to respond with a basket nearly every time the Sixers scored a big one, but they couldn't get stops with LeBron locked in. They ran the same play just about every time down the floor, having Maxey come up to screen for LeBron while Embiid had the ball, and it got LeBron open every time.

Herro missed an open floater down six with a minute, which seemed like it would put the Bucks away for good. Not only did Embiid come up empty on both Sixers possessions, but the Bucks scored quick off those misses, cutting it back down to two with 12 seconds left. The Sixers hurried to beat the Bucks down the floor and Turner had to foul Embiid, picking up his sixth of the game. Embiid buried both free throws, but Brayden Burries stepped back into the corner, burying the open three to cut it to one with 4.8 to go.

Embiid went way into the backcourt on the inbound to take three seconds off the clock before being fouled. It was hard to tell from press row, but he definitely stepped out of bounds running towards the sideline and got lucky the refs missed it. He made both free throws to make it a three-point game with 1.5 seconds left and the Bucks had one timeout left to advance. The Sixers put Barlow in for Embiid. The Bucks sent Herro down the other end of the court who took a dribble before getting his three up. He buried the shot, silencing the crowd, but replay showed that he just didn't get it off in time, making the Sixers very lucky to escape this one in regulation.

#2995. Ime Udoka calls Rockets 'mentally fragile' after loss to Nuggets

After a 7-1 start to the season, the Rockets have gone 5-4 — including a loss to Denver on Friday night — with the 13th-ranked defense in the NBA.

After that loss to the Nuggets, Rockets coach Ime Udoka called his team "mentally fragile" and had more to say (quote via Varun Shankar of the Houston Chronicle).

"There's no effort or any attention to detail on the defensive end at all.... They had four 30-point quarters... you're not going to win anything and that just shows a lack of effort, attention to detail or fight on that end. And so, doesn't really matter what we did offensively. Play that poorly, you're not going to win any games."

It seems a little early in the season to call your team soft, but Udoka is an old-school, defense-first, outwork-your-opponent kind of coach, and Houston's effort lately is clearly frustrating him. Also frustrating him was the fact that Nikola Jokic scored 24 points in the second half (although he has done that to a lot of teams).

Things are not dire in Houston, the Rockets had won 4-of-5 before Friday night and still sit as the No. 4 seed in the West with the second-best offense in the NBA. It's a long regular season where the Rockets can work out their defense and get it on point, this is a team that can make a deep run in the West. Which is why Udoka expects more from them than he has seen of late.

#2993. Steelers' Max Iheanachor in spotlight in matchup with Eagles' Makai Lemon

The Pittsburgh Steelers will travel across Pennsylvania to take on the Philadelphia Eagles in what they hope will be a bounce-back NFL Week 11 matchup on Nov. 22 at Lincoln Financial Field.

First-year coach Mike McCarthy's team struggled on both sides of the ball in a Week 10 blowout loss to the AFC North rival Cincinnati Bengals. Pittsburgh now trails the division race by a game.

Now the Steelers will look to stay close in the standings by beating a typically elite Eagles team that has gotten off to an uncharacteristically slow start to 2026 with a 2-7 record.

Here's USA TODAY's look at storylines to watch as the action unfolds.

Required reading: Steelers' veteran CB was torched by Joe Burrow in loss to Bengals: PFF grades

The Steelers infamously called Lemon to tell him they were selecting him with the No. 21 overall pick in the NFL draft in Pittsburgh this past April. That was before the Eagles swooped in to take him at No. 20 via trade.

Optically, it was a major embarrassment for general manager Omar Khan's front office, but his pivot to selecting his now starting right tackle out of Arizona State instead has not been.

Iheanachor earned his spot in the lineup by Week 3, and his arrival coincided with quarterback Aaron Rodgers, running back Jaylen Warren and the offense having a lot more success than they did in the first two games, when they scored just a single offensive touchdown.

The weeks since have included their growing pains. Iheanachor has allowed five sacks, four QB hits and 21 total pressures in seven games, per Pro Football Focus data. Evaluators with that scouting website give him a 66.7 grade for the season, however. That makes him already a substantial upgrade over Broderick Jones, the 2023 first-round pick who started the past three years but never posted a grade above 58.7 before being traded to Dallas in the preseason.

Lemon is Philadelphia's leading receiver with 37 receptions for 312 yards and a touchdown. That puts him only slightly ahead of Steelers second-round pick Germie Bernard statistically. The latter has 32 catches for 284 yards and a score.

Now we'll get to see both on the same field in South Philly, so the subplot of which player performs better will be an intriguing one.

Receivers Roman Wilson and Michael Pittman Jr. were both designated to return from injured reserve, opening a 21-day window in which they can practice and eventually be added back to the active roster.

The passing game needs them back. Top receiver DK Metcalf has been mostly neutralized in the past two games. His two catches against the Bengals came after the game was well out of reach. He also has among the most drops in the NFL. Bernard, meanwhile, has just 30 yards to his name in that same span.

Pittman was acquired in an offseason trade to help draw coverage away from Metcalf while contributing reliable No. 2 production of his own, but he's only seen the field in three games this season.

Wilson is the speedy separator quarterback Aaron Rodgers could use for some of the downfield throws that simply haven't been hitting lately, save for DK Metcalf's long catch in the win against New Orleans in Paris.

If either or both play, keep an eye on how quickly they're able to get up to speed and change the calculus for Rodgers.

The first-year defensive coordinator's unit failed the test of facing Bengals quarterback Joe Burrow and dangerous receivers Ja'Marr Chase and Tee Higgins on pretty much all fronts.

The Steelers had previously done a nice job of taking away explosive pass plays and forcing quarterbacks to beat them with consistency underneath. Burrow did the latter while also beating Asante Samuel Jr. for long passes to Chase on multiple occasions to make matters much worse.

The concern for the Steelers now is whether that was a product of Burrow's individual brilliance, or whether the scheme may be getting exposed a bit now that the league has had some time to react to it.

The Eagles present an interesting challenge in that regard. Super Bowl-winning quarterback Jalen Hurts is on injured reserves because of blood clots. Joe Milton will make the start in this game instead with little NFL experience to draw from.

He has useful targets to work with, though, in the form of Lemon, fellow receiver Devonta Smith, tight end Dallas Goedert and, of course, the incomparable running back Saquon Barkley.

The Steelers can't take this team lightly two years removed from winning a Super Bowl. And not with the playbook for success against them that they put on tape last week.

#2991. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#2984. Tyler Herro scores 50, officials miss late out-of-bounds call on Joel Embiid in 76ers' 132-129 win over Bucks

Tyler Herro scored a career-high 50 points Saturday, but that wasn't enough for the Milwaukee Bucks to get their second win of the season in a 132-129 defeat to the Philadelphia 76ers.

Milwaukee was deprived of an opportunity to take the lead in the closing seconds when officials missed Joel Embiid stepping out of bounds.

Rookie Brayden Burries hit a corner 3-pointer for the Bucks to cut the Sixers' lead to 130-129. After a timeout, LeBron James threw an inbounds pass to Embiid, who attempted to dribble out the clock near the Milwaukee basket. Embiid stepped out of bounds at the baseline with his left foot, but the officials did not call the turnover and Herro was forced to foul.

The Bucks could not challenge the non-call because they were out of replay challenges.

Embiid made his two free throws to put Philadelphia ahead by 3, but the Bucks still had 1.5 seconds remaining to try for a tying basket.

Herro received the inbounds pass from Kyle Kuzma at the top of the arc and took one dribble to break free from VJ Edgecombe. Shooting from the right elbow, Herro's 3-pointer went in. However, the shot left his hand after the buzzer.

The eighth-year guard shot 15 for 23 from the floor, including 7 of 13 on 3-pointers. Herro also hit all 11 of his free throws, adding 11 rebounds and seven assists. Burries finished with 26 points off the bench with 8 rebounds and four assists. Kuzma was the third Bucks player to reach double figures, scoring 12 with five boards.

Herro's previous career-high was 41 points, reached in a December 2022 win over the Houston Rockets while he was with the Miami Heat.

Milwaukee lost its 13th consecutive game to drop to 1-16 on the season, the worst mark in the NBA. The Bucks' only win came nearly a month ago on Oct. 26, a 110-102 victory over the Indiana Pacers. Up next for them is the Toronto Raptors (6-10) at home on Monday.

Embiid led the Sixers with 26 points, nearly registering a triple-double with 17 rebounds and nine assists. James added 21 points with 6 boards, 6 assists and 3 steals. Seven Philadelphia players scored in double figures, with Anfernee Simons getting 18 off the bench and Tyrese Maxey tallying 17 points.

The 76ers improved to 14-3 with the win, tying the Los Angeles Lakers for the NBA's top record. Philadelphia has won five consecutive games and next faces the Portland Trail Blazers (10-5) on Monday.

#2983. Are the Packers playing today? NFL Week 11 schedule, next game,

The Green Bay Packers could not have picked a better week to rest.

Green Bay is off in Week 11, and after what the Minnesota Vikings did to Jordan Love last week, nobody in that locker room is complaining. In 56 starts, Love had never been sacked more than five times. Minnesota got him eight times on Nov. 15.

The 30-10 loss at Lambeau Field left the Packers 6-4 and alone at the bottom of the NFC North. Love finished 17-of-27 passing for 220 yards with a touchdown and an interception. Green Bay turned it over four times, ran for 56 yards and never led. Kirk Cousins, five days into his second stint with Minnesota after a deadline trade with the Las Vegas Raiders, threw two touchdown passes.

The bye buys Love a week to heal and LaFleur a week to fix his line. The Packers are back on the field Nov. 25, the night before Thanksgiving, against the Los Angeles Rams.

#2978. Are the Seahawks playing today? NFL Week 11 schedule, next game

Sam Darnold has spent the last two Seattle Seahawks games on the sideline. The bye gives him an extra week to get off it.

Seattle is off in Week 11, and the break lands at the right time. Darnold hurt his ribs and quad in the Nov. 2 loss to the Chicago Bears and hasn't played since. The Seahawks are optimistic he'll be back when they visit San Francisco on Nov. 29.

Drew Lock has kept the defending Super Bowl champions afloat. He beat the Arizona Cardinals 17-3, then threw for 240 yards and two touchdowns in a 21-13 win over the Las Vegas Raiders on Nov. 15. Lock is 3-0 as a starter this season. He also rallied Seattle past New England in the opener after Darnold got hurt on the first drive. He still hasn't thrown an interception.

In Las Vegas, Jaxon Smith-Njigba did most of the damage. He caught eight passes for 171 yards, and his 71-yard touchdown in the third quarter put the game away. He was over 1,000 receiving yards by Seattle's eighth play.

The defense sacked Raiders rookie Fernando Mendoza six times in his first NFL start. It hasn't allowed a 100-yard rusher in 39 straight games, playoffs included.

The line needs the week, too. Left tackle Charles Cross (ankle) and left guard Quenton Nelson (knee) sat out in Las Vegas, and right tackle Abraham Lucas got hurt in the second quarter.

At 6-4, with three wins in four games, Seattle is still 2 1/2 games behind the 8-1 49ers in the NFC West. The Seahawks are eighth in the NFC, one spot outside the playoff field, with the same record as the Green Bay Packers and Los Angeles Rams.

The 49ers beat them 32-29 in Seattle in October. They get another shot after the bye.

#2977. Rapid Recap: 76ers 132, Bucks 129

The best team in the East, the Philadelphia 76ers, beat the reeling Milwaukee Bucks, 132-129. The win means the 76ers increase their win streak to five, while the loss means the Bucks increase their losing streak to 13. Despite the loss, it was a valiant effort by the Bucks, with Tyler Herro scoring a career-high 50 points and Brayden Burries posting a career-high 26, but Joel Embiid's 26-point, 17-rebound double-double proved too much.

Following LeBron starting the game with a vintage hammer, Tyler Herro (going back to the headband) quickly gave the Bucks their first points with a three. This back-and-forth from the stars continued for a bit, until Herro took the upper hand going into the first stoppage in the first quarter, at 6:24, with another three—the Bucks holding a 15-8 advantage. This seven-point advantage got trimmed to one, with the 76ers getting what they desired in the paint, taking a 21-20 lead. Although Jabari Walker started to get in on the paint punishment for the Sixers, Kel'el Ware jams and Herro's deep threat weathered the storm. The Bucks took a 30-29 lead into the second quarter, on the shoulders of Headband Herro's 15 points.

The second quarter began with more rim-wrecking and traded buckets, with Ware continuing to be a presence in the restricted area and LeBron going showtime to Dominick Barlow and VJ Edgecombe. At the second's first stoppage, at 6:41, the game remained close but in Milwaukee's hands, 43-40. After being a game-time decision and held quiet early, Tyrese Maxey showed up in the second quarter, scoring six points in a little over three minutes; however, Herro remained hot to neutralise him—20 points with 2:34 remaining in the half! An and-one late in the quarter for Joel Embiid would give the Sixers a 60-59 lead going into the break.

Again, Herro gave the Bucks their first bucket of the half, and Ryan Rollins followed that up with a fast-break lay. But LeBron continued to exploit the Bucks' struggling interior defense with a one-handed slam (even if he showed his age—this was no signature tomahawk). While the Bucks took a 68-63 lead, they lost it before the third's first break at 7:58, going down 70-71. Herro tried to counter Embiid's quick double-double, but between Kyle Kuzma shooting below 30% from the field and Myles Turner hucking threes at a 0/5 clip, the game began to slip away. 83-73 Sixers. Or was it? Herro's 35 after three had the Bucks within striking distance, down just six, 92-98, going into the fourth.

With five unanswered points less than two minutes into the quarter, Barlow forced the Bucks into a quick timeout. 103-92 Sixers. However, Headband Herro wouldn't quit! He'd have a 40-ball with eight minutes left. This, in turn, forced the Sixers into a timeout, as their lead was cut to three, 106-103. The end of the game went blow for blow, Herro and Brayden Burries vs. Embiid and LeBron, but age, a missed out-of-bounds call, and a waived-off buzzer-beater helped the Sixers prevail. What a heartbreaker, but what a game!

Tyler Herro scored 50 points on 15/23 shooting! While it didn't give the Bucks the win, it kept them within fighting distance of the top team in the East. It also could have been more if his game-tying buzzer-beater wasn't called off.

Split Myles Turner's minutes amongst Kel'el Ware and Jericho Sims. At least they grab boards and have awareness of their three-point capabilities.

#2972. Federal Reserve Board releases results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 10:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#2971. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

Deshaun Watson, in the last year of his monster contract, has thrown for 2,118 yards through ten games along with seven touchdowns and four interceptions. He has added 266 yards on the ground with two rushing scores.

The quarterback was put under concussion protocol back in the Week 8 rivalry game against the Pittsburgh Steelers after a helmet-to-helmet hit. He sat out the next week's matchup and Shedeur Sanders took the reins in an overtime loss to the New Orleans Saints. Watson then returned in Week 10 for a win over the Houston Texans.

On the defense, Denzel Ward has three interceptions and Mason Graham and Isaiah McGuire each have four sacks.

The Browns are trying to return to the playoffs for the first time since 2023.

#2967. Sixers Bell Ringer: Sixers barely avoid letdown in win over last-place Bucks

Sixers Bell Ringer Season Standings:VJ Edgecombe – 4Jaylen Brown – 3.5Tyrese Maxey – 3.5Joel Embiid – 2LeBron James – 1Anfernee Simons – 1Dean Wade – 1

If you look up 'trap game' in the NBA encyclopedia, it would describe playing a 1:00pm Saturday matinee against an opponent entering play 1-15 on the season. The Sixers looked sluggish early on, trailing the Milwaukee Bucks for most of the first half. However, they then asserted control in the second half, climbing ahead by as many as 13 points for what appeared to be a comfortable, if not blowout win. Things turned in the final two minutes, though, with the Sixers' 10-point lead falling to as little as one. Tyler Herro, who exploded for a game-high 50 points, nearly had an overtime-forcing three-pointer, but he did not get it off before the buzzer. Philadelphia held on for the 132-129 win and are now 12-0 in clutch time contests. It was certainly not the most glorious of wins, but they all count as one. Now for Bell Ringer.

Joel Embiid: 26 points, 17 rebounds, 9 assists, 1 block, 1 turnover

PHILADELPHIA, PA – NOVEMBER 21: Joel Embiid #21 of the Philadelphia 76ers drives to the basket during the game against the Milwaukee Bucks on November 21, 2026 at Xfinity Mobile Arena in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and/or using this Photograph, user is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. Mandatory Copyright Notice: Copyright 2026 NBAE (Photo by Jesse D. Garrabrant/NBAE via Getty Images) | NBAE via Getty Images

It was not an exemplary shooting performance from Joel Embiid, who shot just 7-of-17 from the field. Every other part of his game was dynamite, however. The big man looked healthy as a very active participant on the glass, putting bodies on guys and hoovering every loose ball in sight. He had tied his season-high for rebounds midway through the third quarter and finished with a season-high 17 boards. Offensively, he made all the right reads from the high post, finding cutting teammates for a number of easy buckets and finishing one assist shy of the triple-double. Embiid began to get Myles Turner in foul trouble in the second half, altogether going 12-of-14 from the foul line, including some clutch makes in the final minute.

LeBron James: 21 points, 6 rebounds, 6 assists, 3 steals, 1 block, 1 turnover

PHILADELPHIA, PENNSYLVANIA – NOVEMBER 21: LeBron James #23 of the Philadelphia 76ers drives against Brayden Burries #0 of the Milwaukee Bucks during the second half of a game at Xfinity Mobile Arena on November 21, 2026 in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania. NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and or using this photograph, User is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. (Photo by Emilee Chinn/Getty Images) | Getty Images

Saturday wasn't a game that will go on a season highlight reel for LeBron, certainly not a career one. But he was such a steadying presence on both ends of the court. Defensively, he orchestrated the switches and created deflections to jumpstart a number of fast breaks. Down the stretch, it was once again James with the ball in his hands, making the right reads to manipulate the defense and ensure the Sixers got good looks at the basket. There are plenty of reasons the Sixers are 12-0 in clutch games this year, one being just plain old luck, but a big one is having one of the greatest players of all-time on the court who has seen everything a defense can throw at you.

Dominick Barlow: 12 points, 4 rebounds, 1 assist, 1 turnover

PHILADELPHIA, PENNSYLVANIA – NOVEMBER 21: Tyler Herro #11 of the Milwaukee Bucks drives against Dominick Barlow #25 of the Philadelphia 76ers during the first half of a game at Xfinity Mobile Arena on November 21, 2026 in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania. Barlow made his season debut after missing the early portion of the season with a back injury. He looked like he hadn't missed a beat, playing his role to perfection in cutting, crashing the offensive glass, and remaining active defensively. Barlow finished 5-of-6 from the field, including one three-pointer, and certainly looks like a guy who will benefit greatly from playing alongside LeBron, who always has his eyes open for someone flitting into open space around the basket. He and Jabari Walker formed a solid frontcourt pairing, with two guys who may not be traditional centers, but can guard multiple positions and keep the defense honest from the perimeter.

#2963. Federal Reserve issues FOMC statement

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 2:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

#2962. Sunday Patriots Notes: Mike Vrabel’s team faces a test of adversity

The New England Patriots are off on a much needed bye week after their season quickly took a turn for the worse over the last two week.

It has been a quieter week than usual here on Pats Pulpit. But still things to clean out the notebook from the week. Welcome to the latest edition of our Sunday Patriots Notes.

As New England Patriots players take their seats in the team auditorium, head coach Mike Vrabel's four pillars of his team identity are plastered on the walls to their left and right.

In Germany last Sunday, those qualities were few and far between as the Patriots suffered another embarrassing defeat overseas, falling 35-10 to the Detroit Lions. The Lions didn't just win on the scoreboard. They also won the battle of effort and finish.

"They played harder than we did, so that's not why they beat us. They certainly outplayed us. They outcoached us, and they played harder," Vrabel said.

It's a troubling trend that has emerged over the last month under Vrabel. Players have acknowledged a lack of energy in back-to-back weeks. Linebacker Christian Elliss pointed to a lack of trust on the defensive side of the ball.

Vrabel's other three pillars — ball security and ball disruption, details, technique and fundamentals, and making great decisions — are also nowhere to be seen. The Patriots rank 31st in the league in turnover differential (-10). The defense has cratered into one of the league's worst units amid persistent issues with technique and fundamentals.

Even after a week in which Vrabel appeared to challenge his players publicly, his message didn't seem to get through. Asked whether he views his group as mentally fragile, Vrabel didn't directly answer.

"Again, adversity is a part of professional sports," Vrabel said. "So, I've shown opportunities where we've responded … The consistency of that and understanding and showing them one example of saying, 'Here you are stopping them four plays and getting an interception,' and on the other side, 'Here's just not good enough run defense and allows them to score.'

"So, I don't know which one it is. I just know that we need the more positive actions and the ones that we believe that somebody's going to make a play as opposed to what could possibly go wrong."

The Patriots will face another significant test of their ability to respond to adversity after quarterback Drake Maye suffered an ankle injury that will keep him out indefinitely. Vrabel noted that Maye will not be available coming out of the team's bye week, leaving backup quarterback Tommy DeVito to take over the offense.

At 5-5, however, the Patriots still find themselves in the playoff race with everything in front of them. Their season is far from over, but they'll need to show an ability to overcome adversity that has been missing for much of the year.

"When they make a play, it's our response that's critical. We can't let one play affect the next one," Vrabel said.

Sunday's loss in Germany offered another example of that problem. After Jahmyr Gibbs broke off a 50-yard reception, exposing season-long issues with both the Patriots' coverage and pass rush, the defense offered little resistance on the next play as Gibbs scored on a five-yard handoff.

"If they make a play, they make a play, and I think that those are things that happened to us the other day, things that we did to ourselves," Vrabel said. "If they make a really nice play, a contested catch or they break a tackle, we've got to line up again and go play. Sometimes we let the play before, unfortunately, determine maybe what happens on the next play."

For the Patriots to get back on track, it starts with Vrabel.

The head coach must find a way to revive his struggling defense, particularly with a backup quarterback now leading the offense. But just as importantly, he needs to get his team playing to the standard he has set since arriving in New England.

The four pillars plastered on the walls of the team auditorium and emphasized in meetings mean little if they aren't showing up on the field on Sundays. Vrabel has made it clear what he expects from his players. Now, he needs to find a way to get them to respond when things don't go their way.

The Patriots have seven games remaining to show they can do just that.

New England currently sits as the AFC's No. 8 seed, with a 36% chance of making the playoffs. If the Patriots show fight and make a push down the stretch, they'll likely need to surpass the six-win Browns or Steelers.

The most realistic path to putting themselves in playoff position would be winning their three games against the Chargers, Jets and Dolphins while stealing at least one win against the Bills, Vikings, Chiefs or Broncos.

If the season goes in the other direction, the Patriots will begin turning their attention toward their draft position in what continues to project as an extremely talented 2027 class. At 5-5, New England currently holds the No. 16 overall pick, with 12 teams sitting at three wins or fewer.

Last season, teams that finished with five wins held the sixth and seventh overall picks, likely representing the best-case scenario for where New England could select in the first round. Other potential draft positions based on final win totals include:

The Patriots have two weeks to get quarterback Tommy DeVito ready for his first start in Los Angeles in Week 12. It will be interesting to see how offensive coordinator Josh McDaniels adjusts his offense with DeVito under center instead of Drake Maye, with the first though being a less aggressive downfield passing attack, considering Maye ranks fourth in the league in average depth of target (10.1 yards).

DeVito showcased an impressive deep ball during training camp this summer, but it would be unrealistic to expect him to replicate the explosiveness Maye brings to the passing game.

One positive moving forward is that there were plenty of opportunities for DeVito to make plays on tape against the Lions, as New England's pass catchers consistently found ways to get open. DeVito struggled seeing the field and with ball placement, however, areas the Patriots will hope to see improve now that he has gotten his first extensive regular-season action since December 2024.

"I think there were some things that he missed, but also not having a ton of live action since training camp, and really, I think he'll be ready to go coming out of the bye," Vrabel said. "I think that he'll be able to look at some things and understand, whether that's being able to transfer up into the pocket, get us in the right play, get the ball out quicker or look at protections or rotation, whatever that may be. I think that'll all improve."

The Patriots have used five of their eight return designations from injured reserve this season, leaving them with three remaining for the rest of the regular season. Barring a setback, left guard Alijah Vera-Tucker is on track to take one of those spots. Eligible to return next week, Vera-Tucker has ramped up his rehab in recent weeks as he works his way back from a hamstring injury.

Wide receiver Romeo Doubs is also eligible to return next week after suffering a scapula injury in Week 6.

Patriots' second round pick Gabe Jacas continued to be one of the team's few bright spots last Sunday. Jacas tallied two pressures, one being a clean-up sack, and also discarding All-Pro left tackle Penei Sewell to make one of his three run stops.

On the year, Jacas ranks second among rookies in pressures (28), trailing only No. 2 overall pick David Bailey, and leads rookie edge defenders in run stops.

"Now you're getting to this thing where you're getting a little bit banged up. We're about halfway through the season. College ball's starting to finish up, and he's consistent. He's got his routine. He's staying on his routine, which is big," outside linebackers coach Mike Smith said.

"And he's getting comfortable, right? He's out there. He's communicating more. He's talking more. It's starting to slow down a little bit more. Still got a ways to go, but he's just feeling comfortable.

The Patriots will be back in the New Balance Athletics Center on Monday as they fully turn the page to the Los Angeles Chargers. The schedule will then be slightly altered with Thanksgiving on Thursday.

This means Mike Vrabel media availability on Monday will be followed by the first practice of the week on Tuesday. Players will have a typical Wednesday and Friday but will be off for Thanksgiving. New England will then head out to the west coast on Saturday where they will visit the Chargers on Sunday at 4:05 p.m. ET.

#2959. What we learned as Keegan Murray game-winner snaps Kings' historic home skid

What we learned as Keegan Murray game-winner snaps Kings' historic home skid originally appeared on NBC Sports Bay Area

SACRAMENTO — It came down to their final possession for the second straight night for the Kings on Saturday at Golden 1 Center.

Trailing 96-95 to the Boston Celtics with 11.8 seconds left, Darius Acuff Jr. missed a driving layup. But Keegan Murray was there for the offensive rebound over former teammate Devin Carter, and his put back gave Sacramento a 97-96 lead over the team with the second-best record in the Eastern Conference.

Boston had a chance to win it at the end. But Murray stayed glued to Celtics guard Payton Pritchard and forced a tough miss, giving the Kings their first home win of the 2026-7 NBA season.

And it was Acuff's first time lighting the beam as the Kings snapped the longest home winless streak to begin a season in franchise history at nine games. Sacramento improved to 2-14 on the season.

Acuff led the Kings with 21 points on 7-of-23 shooting, while De'Andre Hunter had 20 points and six rebounds.

For Boston, they got a season-high 19 points from Mike Conley Jr. and 19 points with eight rebounds from Paul George. The Celtics shot just 33.7 percent while missing star Jayson Tatum, who was given the night off for rest.

Murray's five-year, $140 million contract he signed last November kicked in this season. And to this point, there were questions as to whether the Kings made a mistake by inking Murray to such a lucrative long-term deal.

He came into the game averaging 10.3 points while shooting just 25 percent from 3-point range, and had been dealing with ankle capsulitis stemming from an injury last season.

Murray finished Saturday's game with 15 points and 12 rebounds, and made the game's two biggest plays.

Sacramento was sloppy offensively after playing Friday night against the Los Angeles Lakers. They allowed the Celtics to take a 54-41 lead into halftime despite Boston shooting just 37.3 percent.

That was because the Celtics had 16 more shot attempts, thanks to Sacramento's nine turnovers leading to 14 points. Domantas Sabonis had five by himself, with four coming during a rough stint early in the second quarter.

Sabonis finished with 15 points, 17 rebounds and a game-high seven turnovers. Nine of his points came in the third quarter. He helped Sacramento outscore the Celtics 56-42 after halftime.

The Kings cut Boston's lead to just two while starting the second half on a 17-6 run. The run came in contrast to Sacramento's season-long trend of being the NBA's worst third-quarter team.

They came into the game ranked dead last in third-quarter point differential at minus-88 in 15 games.

Sacramento tied the game at 60 on a fast-break dunk from Leaky Black. And Acuff gave the Kings the lead with a pull-up 3 moments later.

In all, the Kings outscored the Celtics 34-13 in the frame and took a nine-point lead into the fourth. Boston made just 5 of 27 shots (18.5 percent) while missing all 13 of their 3-pointers.

Four Kings starters – Acuff, Hunter, Zach LaVine and Sabonis – played all 12 minutes in the third.

The Kings up next will host the San Antonio Spurs on Monday at Golden 1 Center.

#2956. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2946. 3 alternative starting lineups the Suns should consider

As the Phoenix Suns move into ninth place in the Western Conference, riding a 3-0 record on their current road trip, you might think everything is hunky-dory. That this team is finally gelling in a way that's translating into victories. And I'm not going to argue against that. I think there's merit to it. But that doesn't mean everything is right in Phoenix. It means this team is finding its footing and taking advantage of some inferior competition, as well it should. That's what good teams do.

One thing continues to be clear, however. The starting five that Phoenix trots onto the court every night remains one of the worst in the league.

And I know, I know. It's not about who starts. It's about who finishes. That's something I firmly believe, although when your starters aren't getting the job done, you're forced to rely on your bench to stabilize things. That's hardly ideal. Thankfully for Phoenix, they possess one of the most productive bench units in the NBA, a testament to the team's overall depth.

Still, when your starting unit consistently fails to set the table, you're going to struggle against quality opponents. We've seen that play out throughout the early portion of this season. Starting matters, despite my personal philosophy on the subject. Especially when you factor in the egos of players and the agents who represent them. If someone believes they should be starting, particularly when they're being compensated like a starter, you can bet that any suggestion of moving them to the bench will be met with resistance from an agent who believes his client's value is being diminished.

One thing working in Phoenix's favor has been relative health. I know that might sound strange considering the injury to Mark Williams, but holistically, the starting unit has enjoyed considerable continuity through the first 15 games. In fact, only two teams in the NBA have utilized only two different starting lineups this season. The New York Knicks and the Phoenix Suns.

That continuity hasn't necessarily translated into production, however. When you examine the numbers associated with Phoenix's two starting lineups, they're pretty abysmal. The unit featuring Khaman Maluach has posted a net rating of -11.5 across nine games together. The lineup featuring Oso Ighodaro? An even worse -14.4.

If we're looking for ways to tweak the Suns' approach, exploring changes to the starting lineup might be where the answer lies. Whether that means adding playmaking, improving the rebounding, or injecting a little more attitude into the mix, Phoenix might benefit from experimenting with different combinations if it wants to compete more consistently against the league's better teams.

Now, I understand the importance of establishing continuity. The more time these players spend together, the better they'll understand where their teammates are supposed to be on both ends of the floor. That familiarity builds cohesion, and cohesion can eventually translate into success. So I'm not necessarily advocating for an immediate change, especially with the Suns playing quality basketball and winning seven of their last nine games.

But there comes a point when you've seen enough from a particular lineup to understand its strengths, its limitations, and what it ultimately provides. When that point arrives, it might behoove Jordan Ott to explore some alternatives. With that in mind, here are three starting lineups the Phoenix Suns could experiment with.

I'll start simple here by adding Jordan Goodwin to the starting five.

Goodwin has been stellar this season, and when you look at his on/off metrics, the Suns have been better in nearly every category, including defensive rebounding percentage. His net rating differential sits at +20.7, the ninth-best mark in the league.

Now, you can look at that two ways. The first is that he clearly has a positive impact whenever he steps onto the floor. You feel it. You see it. He's one of those emotional players who has the ability to affect everyone around him, like that positive coworker who walks into the office and immediately makes the day better. Everybody enjoys playing alongside Goodwin because of the effort he provides.

The other way to interpret that number, and probably the way I lean, is that the starting lineup has been so bad that Goodwin benefits from being part of a second unit that consistently outperforms it. When the group you're replacing struggles as much as Phoenix's starters have, it's easier to generate an impressive net rating differential.

The interesting question is where Goodwin fits if you move him into the starting lineup. My choice? I'd replace Dillon Brooks, primarily because Goodwin provides a greater rebounding presence than Brooks currently does. Brooks is averaging 3.6 rebounds per game, while Goodwin is pulling down 4.7. From a rebounding percentage perspective, Brooks sits at 6.1%, compared to Goodwin's 10.9%.

If rebounding is an issue for the starting unit, which it certainly is, adding Goodwin helps address that deficiency while providing a player whose skill set complements what Miles Bridges brings to the floor rather than overlapping with it.

It's also worth noting that a lineup featuring Devin Booker, Jalen Green, Jordan Goodwin, Miles Bridges, and Oso Ighodaro has already appeared for eight minutes this season. The net rating for that combination? +37.5. Yes, it's a small sample size. But within that limited opportunity, the results have been pretty damn good.

If there's one guy I'd really like to see get going, it's Luke Kennard. Through 14 games, he's averaging 17.3 minutes per night, providing 8.6 points, 1.7 rebounds, and 1.6 assists. His shooting? It's been as advertised, connecting on 43.8% of his three-point attempts. The issue is that he's attempting only 4.6 threes per game.

That was one of the concerns when Kennard arrived in Phoenix. Would he be willing and able to become a volume shooter? Because ultimately, that's what the Suns needed him to be. You're replacing the three-point production of Grayson Allen and Royce O'Neale, two players who certainly weren't afraid to let it fly. Allen averaged 8.9 three-point attempts per game last season. O'Neale? 6.7.

Perhaps one way to stoke the flame of what Kennard could become is to insert him into the starting five.

His efficiency has been stellar, although you'd be sacrificing some of what Jalen Green does well and probably doesn't receive enough credit for, most notably his rebounding and defensive length. In return, you're adding another legitimate perimeter threat alongside Dillon Brooks, who has been fantastic from deep to start the season.

You'd have Devin Booker handling much of the facilitation and distribution, although Kennard has demonstrated an ability to contribute in that department as well. He doesn't do it in volume, because quite frankly, nothing he's doing right now comes in volume. But his 12.3% assist percentage isn't all that far removed from Green's 17.3%.

Perhaps the most telling statistic comes from the on/off numbers. In the 325 minutes Jalen Green has been on the court without Luke Kennard, the Suns have posted a net rating of -9.8. When Kennard is on the floor without Green? Across 165 minutes, that number skyrockets to +26.9.

That's a pretty significant difference, and one that makes me curious about what Kennard could provide with an expanded role. I'd be willing to gamble a little and see what that looks like.

All right, I'm going to give you the one you've been waiting for, and you're probably surprised I didn't list it first. Yeah, I toyed with you a bit. Can't bury the lead, you know?

In my opinion, this is one of the most obvious options for the Phoenix Suns. Jalen Green has been inefficient, and much of what he provides offensively overlaps with Devin Booker. If you can somehow convince Green (and his agent, Andrew Morrison) that moving to the bench is a good idea, the Suns could ultimately benefit. And so could Green. If he embraces that sixth man role, becoming the guy who comes in and uses his athleticism to score against opposing second units, it could actually increase his value over the long term. Or it could completely disrupt what has become one of the most productive second units in the league. I don't know.

But when you look toward the future, knowing that Collin Gillespie is likely to be around longer than Green and continue playing alongside Devin Booker, it might behoove the Suns to give him more opportunities to develop that relationship on the court.

Thus far this season, Gillespie and Booker have played 200 minutes together, posting a net rating of -7.7. Clearly, there's room for improvement, and perhaps that improvement begins by giving Gillespie an opportunity with the starting five.

Now, you might be wondering why I'm suggesting starting Khaman Maluach over Oso Ighodaro in this scenario. It's simple. What Oso does well is facilitate from the center position, utilizing high screens and dribble handoffs to create opportunities and open up different aspects of the offense. When you have someone like Gillespie, who can effectively navigate an offense with the ball in his hands, you don't necessarily need that additional wrinkle in your starting five. What you could use, however, is more effective rebounding and shot blocking. That's where Maluach comes into the equation.

We've actually seen this combination on the court this season, although it has existed for only six minutes. But what a glorious six minutes it was. The Suns posted a net rating of +92.3 with that lineup. Clearly, that's not a statistic you can live and die by given the limited sample size, but if you're thinking about the future of this team, getting Gillespie and Maluach opportunities to develop together could be highly beneficial.

Another reason I'm not advocating for Oso in this particular lineup is that we've already seen what that combination looks like, albeit in another limited sample. Across 10 minutes featuring Gillespie, Booker, Brooks, Bridges, and Ighodaro, the Suns posted a net rating of -47.6.

Again, we're dealing with extremely small sample sizes, but I think that further fans the flames of my thesis. Having Oso in a starting lineup alongside Gillespie potentially diminishes some of what makes Ighodaro effective and valuable to this team. His ability to facilitate becomes less essential when another capable ball handler is already providing that element.

Of the three lineups I've explored, this is the one I'd most like to see the Suns experiment with. That doesn't necessarily mean Gillespie and Maluach need to become permanent starters. But at some point this season, I'd like to see them share the floor more frequently so we can gather meaningful data and determine whether those early numbers hold up. And you best believe I'll be keeping an eye on it.

Those are my lineups. Those are my suggestions. Again, they don't necessarily need to be starting lineups. It's fun to suggest three different starting fives that the Suns should trot onto the court, but in reality, we know that's unlikely to happen for the reasons I mentioned above. Agents, egos, and contracts ultimately determine which players get introduced at the beginning of games, with fireworks going off and a gorilla waving a giant flag.

That being said, there's an opportunity for head coach Jordan Ott to weave some of these combinations into games, giving his coaching staff a chance to evaluate how productive they can be. As you can see, there's very little data available on some of these lineups, which leaves plenty of room for speculation. The best way to eliminate that speculation is to actually put these combinations on the court and see what happens. That provides meaningful data, helps us better understand what works and what doesn't, and ultimately gives the coaching staff more information to make decisions that may eventually become necessary.

The Suns have won seven of their last nine games, and that's encouraging. But winning shouldn't prevent Jordan Ott from exploring ways to make this team better. The starting lineup has consistently struggled, and there are intriguing alternatives worth investigating. Maybe none of these combinations provides the answer. Maybe one changes everything. We won't know until they're given meaningful opportunities. Because continuity is valuable, but at some point, continuing to do something that isn't working becomes stubbornness rather than strategy.

#2944. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.
