#2876. Nvidia stock plunges 14%, wraps up longest weekly losing streak since 2008 financial crisis

The last time Wall Street was this down on Nvidia the chipmaker's flagship product was the GeForce graphics processor for high-end PC video games, and markets were reeling from the global financial crisis.

Nvidia shares tumbled 14% on Friday, sliding for a seventh straight day as investors grew increasingly concerned about life after co-founder Jensen Huang. The stock closed lower for a seventh consecutive week, the longest such stretch since 2008.

Shares of Nvidia are down 46% over the period, pushing the company's market cap down by about $2.6 trillion. The stock is now trading at roughly 8.4 times forward earnings, while Advanced Micro Devices is valued at 29 times profit for the next 12 months.

On Friday, Reuters reported that the Securities and Exchange Commission is exploring potential accounting issues at Nvidia, pushing the stock down further.

Once the most valuable company in the world, Nvidia now has a market cap of $3 trillion, putting it fourth behind Apple, Microsoft and Alphabet.

Nvidia is reeling from the removal of Huang, who resigned on Nov. 12, after multiple reported affairs, including one with a Chinese businesswoman who was allegedly a spy.

Harvey Jones, Nvidia's newly appointed board chair, said earlier this week that Huang had resigned after "disclosure of personal behavior inconsistent with the company's code of conduct, standards and cultural norms."

But the chipmaker's losing streak started before Nvidia was thrown into crisis as investors worried over the sustainability of the AI buildout, particularly after the midterm elections, and emerging competition in AI chips from Google and AMD.

Even a better-than-expected earnings report, which included an increase to fiscal 2028 guidance, didn't stem the selling. Interim CEO Colette Kress, the company's longtime CFO, projected revenue growth next fiscal year of 75%, and said that customers aren't going anywhere.

On Tuesday's earnings call with analysts, Kress signed off by saying, "we've got this." In a company meeting the next day, executives told staff that it was moving its yearly review process, which can come with more stock, from March to December.

For investors, one major question is whether Nvidia can continue to innovate and sustain its commanding lead in the AI chip market without its visionary leader. Almost the entire company now is built around the AI trade, a phenomenon that began taking hold just four years ago with the launch of OpenAI's ChatGPT.

Before that, Nvidia's primary business was selling graphics cards used for video games. In mid-2008, the last time the stock was this deep into a weekly losing streak, Nvidia was generating less than $1 billion per quarter. Now it pulls in more than $1 billion per day. At the time, the entire market was in freefall due to the worst financial crisis since the Great Depression.

#2875. Is a bottom in for the Nasdaq-100? Charts to watch

(See the video above for Frank's complete chart breakdown.)

Is the Nasdaq-100's recent bounce a sign that the bottom is in? We're going to look at several charts to help answer that question.

First, let's remember how we got here. In September, the NDX broke out from a bullish cup-and-handle pattern, producing a 33,200-upside target. That breakout quickly failed, however, and gave way to a bearish pattern that broke down aggressively two weeks ago.

The decline brought the index relatively close to its 26,350 downside target before the current rebound began.

Zooming out, it's telling to see where the bounce originated. Three important forms of support converged in the 25,000-26,000 area:

This confluence of support helped encourage buying after a roughly 15% decline.

For perspective, we also see what larger drawdowns of 20%, 30%, 40%, and 50% would look like … a lot worse.

The following chart tracks NDX drawdowns from 52-week highs going back to just after the financial crisis. Only a handful of declines exceeded 20%, and three produced relatively quick reversals: late 2018, the Covid lows in 2020 and April 2025.

Historically, buying into declines of approximately 15%–25% has generally paid off, even when additional downside occurred first (yellow highlights).

The chart also highlights previous declines of 10%–16% in green, putting the current pullback into perspective.

The major exception was 2022, when the NDX eventually declined nearly 36% over approximately 10 months ... a much worse outcome, which many investors are concerned could happen again now.

That raises the key question: Are we experiencing another routine correction, or the early stages of a larger bear market?

One important clue will be how far this bounce progresses.

Any rally that fails to reclaim the previous highs could establish a significant lower high. Additional lower highs would provide stronger evidence of a developing, longer downtrend.

We saw this in March 2022, when that first sharp rebound effort failed to reach the previous high before a much more severe decline followed.

The bottom line: The current bounce is encouraging, particularly given where it originated and knowing how well buying prior 10-20% drawdowns has paid off.

From here, it's all about the follow through.  We don't need to see new highs right away, but any rally from here that's met with another wave of strong selling could reignite downside momentum.

Frank Cappelleri is the founder and president of CappThesis, an independent research firm that helps active investors through time-tested chart and statistical analysis. #2872. Even John Harbaugh wonders ‘what are we going to do with this opportunity?’

John Harbaugh has coached in 24 playoff games, fifth-most of all-time. His Baltimore Ravens teams made the playoffs in 12 of his 18 seasons as head coach. Patrick Ricard, Isaiah Likely, Jordan Stout, Malik Harrison, and Ar'Darius Washington — who came to the New York Giants this season to join Harbaugh — have been through plenty of annual races to reach the playoffs with Harbaugh.

Aside from the Baltimore transfers, though, there are not a lot of Giants used to these playoff stretch drives.

Kayvon Thibodeaux and Andrew Thomas were on the Giants' 2022 playoff team. Jason Pinnock was on that team, as well as with the San Francisco 49ers for two playoff games last season.

Running back Devin Singletary has been part of five playoff teams.

Backup guard Aaron Stinnie won a Super Bowl with the Tampa Bay Buccaneers in 2021 and has been on three playoff teams.

Guard Jon Runyan Jr. was a part of three Green Bay Packers teams that reached the playoffs.

Reserve cornerback Nic Jones was part of a Super Bowl team with the Kansas City Chiefs in 2024.

Right tackle Jermaine Eluemunor has played in two playoff games, in a 10-year career none since 2022 with the Las Vegas Raiders.

Defensive tackle Shelby Harris has played in two playoff games in a 13-year career, none since 2014.

Safety Jevon Holland and backup quarterback Jameis Winston have been in one playoff game.

Special teamer Elijah Campbell, tight end Chris Manhertz, and cornerback Greg Newsome have two games of playoff experience.

That isn't a lot. Quarterback J.J. McCarthy has won high school and college national championships, but hasn't played an NFL playoff game. What the Giants are about to experience is new to key players like Cam Skattebo, Malik Nabers, Abdul Carter, Arvell Reese, and plenty of others.

Even for those who have done it, playing important games down the strech of an NFL season is a new experience with this group.

"It's part of where we're at right now. We have a team and we're together. The way I look at it, we're a collective. We've got guys who've done things and different things or whatever, different levels of accomplishment, different ages, all those things. But as a collective, we haven't been there in these types of games," Harbaugh said this week. "So, that's our next step. What are we going to do with this opportunity? What are we going to do over the next few weeks? Where are we going to go? It's up to us to make the determination of who we are.

"So, there's a lot at stake, and we'll find out how we're going to handle it. I mean, you don't know. You can talk all you want, you can say all you want, until you get out there and you start doing it and you find out really where you're at. And we're going to learn a lot."

The lessons begin Sunday afternoon against the Jacksonville Jaguars.

The Xavier Gipson Experience begins Sunday for the Giants. It could be great. It could be a disaster. I just know that if you thought you had to hold your breath when the ball was kicked to Charlie Jones, you will be doing the same, maybe worse, with Gipson.

With no injuries keeping players out Sunday, I am curious to see what the Giants' inactives list looks like.

I have two big questions today. Can the Giants run the ball well enough to keep J.J. McCarthy from having to carry the offense with his arm? Who wins the blitz battle between Giants defensive coordinator Dennard Wilson and Jaguars quarterback Trevor Lawrence.

#2868. How China is using summits to expand its global influence

SHENZHEN, China — For a second year in a row, Chinese President Xi Jinping has brought Russian President Vladimir Putin together with another major world leader on mainland Chinese shores.

U.S. President Donald Trump had a trilateral meeting with Xi and Putin on Thursday, after the three met the prior evening during a welcome banquet for the Asia-Pacific Economic Leaders' Meeting in Shenzhen. It marked the first time they had met together in person.

In September 2025, Xi, Putin and Indian Prime Minister Narendra Modi briefly laughed together for a friendly photo-op alongside an annual meeting of the Shanghai Cooperation Organization in Tianjin, China. The gathering followed easing tensions between Beijing and New Delhi.

China's "ability to engage directly with the world major powers" reflects the country's influence across the Global South and Eurasia, said Jodie Wen, postdoctoral fellow at the Center for International Security and Strategy at Tsinghua University. "China hopes to play a more constructive role in this conflict-[ridden], unstable world."

In the future, "China will hope to provide more platforms" within its borders for countries to discuss issues, she said.

Trump, Xi, Putin said they plan to boost oil production, revamp UN after trilateral talks

I watched Trump, Xi and Putin gather in China. Here are my top takeaways

Trump holds talks separately with Putin and Xi ahead of their historic trilateral meeting

Takaichi gets chilly reception in China on first Japan leader visit since 2019

Why the Middle East is watching the Trump-Xi-Putin summit

Trump arrives in China for historic meeting with Xi, Putin

China's Xi calls for removing trade and investment barriers

Putin says EU is losing industry, falling behind competition

Three things to watch as Trump, Xi and Putin meet in China

China has increasingly played the role of broker, for instance, providing the forum for restoring diplomatic ties between Saudi Arabia and Iran in 2023.

By participating in and hosting different multilateral organizations, China has stayed in the Western orbit while cultivating relations with non-Western countries. The two photo-ops last year and this year reflect only a portion of Xi's large number of meetings with different world leaders.

Xi has hosted more than 40 heads of state so far this year, including at least eight around the APEC meetings in Shenzhen this week. That's on top of receiving over 60 state visits in 2025, according to CNBC analysis of official Chinese readouts.

Domestic Chinese media have highlighted how Xi has met with many foreign leaders around the China-hosted events.

"Beijing sees these multilateral platforms as opportunities to expand its international influence and demonstrate its commitment to multilateralism, while bilateral diplomacy remains equally important," said Yue Su, principal economist for China at EIU.

But she noted that different countries increasingly pursue different geopolitical and economic priorities, making it challenging to reach consensus even through multilateral organizations.

"Against this backdrop, China's diplomatic priorities are increasingly focused on delivering tangible economic benefits through trade, investment, financial connectivity and technology cooperation," she said.

On Thursday, Xi announced at the APEC Economic Leaders' Meeting that China would provide $20 million in support for the Asia Pacific region. He also advocated for the development of an "Asia-Pacific community with a shared future," an iteration of his commonly used slogan on a "shared future" for humanity more broadly.

The Shenzhen summit can create meaningful outcomes for crisis management, as well as diplomatic space for future negotiations, Tsinghua University's Wen said. She added that it's important for leaders to come together in person in order to avoid misunderstandings and further tensions.

Trump said Thursday at the trilateral that the U.S., China and Russia are "uniquely positioned" to discuss reducing nuclear weapons at their historic meeting. A Chinese readout subsequently said the three leaders discussed Ukraine, the Middle East and the Korean Peninsula.

—CNBC's Matthew Tan contributed to this report.

India A and Pakistan Shaheens meet in Doha on Sunday with both sides unbeaten after one match apiece, which means the winner takes control of Group B with a game still to come against the loser of the morning fixture between the UAE and Nepal.

India A arrived off the back of the largest winning margin in the history of List A cricket. Tilak Varma made 107 and Kumar Kushagra 117 as they posted 332 for 9 against Nepal on Friday, and Nepal were then bowled out for 26 in reply, with Prince Yadav taking 5 for 6 and Harshit Rana 4 for 16 in a 306-run win.

Pakistan Shaheens opened with a 61-run win over the UAE on the same day, a more ordinary afternoon than India A's but one that leaves the two sides level on two points and separated only by net run rate.

#2864. A trading strategy to ride out the Nvidia uncertainty and generate income in the process

Jensen Huang's abrupt resignation has sent shockwaves through the tech sector, dragging Nvidia's (NVDA) stock down roughly 30% since Oct. 6. The market despises uncertainty, and losing a visionary founder, particularly one without a well-telegraphed succession plan, makes it worse.

However, for opportunistic investors, this panic has made the stock cheaper (obviously) while options have become significantly more expensive. Trading in the $160s, Nvidia's valuation has taken a big haircut, yet its underlying business fundamentals remain largely intact. While Huang was the architect of Nvidia's AI dominance, the company's moat — superior GPUs, the CUDA software ecosystem, and networking expertise — does not walk out the door with him. The company's structural advantage in the global artificial intelligence rollout persists.

Buying good companies at a discount is investing 101, but the current market dynamics may do something else for those who use options.  Huang's departure guarantees that Nvidia will be operating under a cloud of transition for the foreseeable future. Executing a permanent CEO search while navigating global regulatory scrutiny takes time. Consequently, Nvidia's implied volatility (IV)—the market's pricing of expected future price swings — has spiked to highs not seen since the tariff tantrum in the first half of 2025, and this elevated IV is highly likely to persist for several months as the leadership drama slowly unfolds.

This prolonged period of high implied volatility can be a goldmine for option sellers, specifically those executing options overlays such as covered call strategies. When you buy NVDA shares at these depressed levels and simultaneously sell out-of-the-money call options against your position, the premiums you collect are directly tied to IV. Because fear and transition-related uncertainty have dramatically inflated these option prices, the income generated from selling covered calls is now significantly higher than usual.

This lowers the risk profile of holding the stock considerably over time. Consistently collecting these inflated premiums systematically lowers your overall cost basis in the underlying shares. If the stock trades sideways or recovers slowly during the CEO search, you are generating an outsized cash yield on a stagnant asset. If the stock experiences further downside turbulence, the heavy premium income acts as a substantial downside buffer, meaningfully reducing the strategy's overall risk over time.

By deploying a persistent covered call strategy, investors can leverage the exact fear driving the stock down to generate robust, risk-reducing income while maintaining exposure to the undeniable future of AI.

#2862. Newcastle 2-2 Arsenal — Match Report: Wild St. James’ Park Comeback Falls Just Short

Newcastle United fought back from a goal down to draw 2-2 with Premier League leader Arsenal at St James' Park, producing a remarkable second-half transformation.

Harvey Barnes and Malick Thiaw overturned Eberechi Eze's opener before Noni Madueke rescued a point for the visitors with 12 minutes remaining, with the Magpies surrendering points from a winning position.

Arsenal controlled the opening half almost completely. Former Newcastle midfielder Bruno Guimaraes was booed on his return to Tyneside, but the home side struggled to turn that atmosphere into attacking momentum. Madueke struck the post early, while Newcastle failed to log a shot on target before halftime.

The breakthrough arrived in the 33rd minute after Lewis Hall failed to deal with a long ball. Kai Havertz took advantage and released Eze behind the defense, with the midfielder finishing past Lukas Hornicek. Newcastle remained passive afterward and was fortunate to reach halftime only one goal behind.

Matthias Jaissle responded decisively, making three substitutions at the break as Tino Livramento, Jacob Ramsey and Anthony Elanga replaced Amar Dedic, Sean Steur and Jacob Murphy. The changes had an immediate effect.

Martin Odegaard sliced a clearance from a throw-in, William Osula seized possession and found Barnes, who finished past David Raya in the 49th minute.

Newcastle suddenly played with far greater intensity, as Lewis Miley tested Raya before the turnaround was completed in the 62nd minute. Hall curled a corner toward Dan Burn, who was left unmarked and headed the ball down, allowing Thiaw to force it home from close range and send St James' Park into life.

Jaissle's side briefly threatened to extend its advantage, with Joe Willock driving forward and Miley also finding dangerous positions.

Arsenal eventually broke through Newcastle's improved press in the 78th minute, however, and Madueke cut inside from the right before driving a low finish through Burn's legs and beyond Hornicek.

Jaissle was encouraged by the response despite mixed feelings about the result.

"The first half was not good, the second half was excellent, so if you combine these two then I think a point is fair," Jaissle said. "We encouraged them to do the high press in the right way, with a front-footed approach, not hesitating, not doubting, just pure belief in the way we want to do it."

The draw left Newcastle 12th with 13 points and provided a far more encouraging performance after its recent struggles, albeit in another no-win outcome. Jaissle's side now heads south to face another top dog, Brighton, next Sunday.

#2861. Discussion: Is today’s game a must win for the Panthers?

It's rare that an interconference match-up in November has enough stakes to be considered a must win, but the Carolina Panthers might find themselves in a position where that's the case. They're currently just 4-5, but that's enough to lead the NFC South by a game over the Saints and Buccaneers with the Falcons just one extra loss behind. It's a tightly packed race, and by rule, someone has to win the division and make the playoffs.

The Panthers are coming off a key step towards winning the division with a road win over the Saints last week. Up next is the Ravens. Going into the season, this looked like a tough game, probably even penciled in as a loss for the Panthers. But now the Ravens are on their third string quarterback who they just traded for like two weeks ago. The Panthers are favorites, so they should win this game. But the question is, do they have to?

MY TAKE: Yes it is. The Panthers still could win the division with a loss, but their road will be a whole lot tougher for it. After this game, the Panthers will have seven games left on their schedule. Three of those are against NFC South rivals with one game apiece. The Panthers can win all of those. But winning all of those gets them to seven wins, and seven wins seems very unlikely to make the playoffs in a 17 game schedule, no matter how bad the division is. They have to get at least one or two more wins from outside the division from here on out, and this is by far the best chance for them to do it.

The rest of the Panthers non-division schedule looks like this:

This is definitely the Panthers' chance to get a win, and it's very likely it's the only game they'll be favored in. The Steelers are gettable, but the other three teams on this schedule have legitimate Super Bowl aspirations. It's hard to see the Panthers stealing one of those games.

The best path for the Panthers to make the playoffs is to take care of their division games and steal at least one more game outside the division. The rest of the non-divisional schedule is brutal, so that makes today a must win game, in my opinion.

#2860. Celtics announce new starting lineup against Kings in wake of injuries

SACRAMENTO — The Celtics will start Payton Pritchard, Hugo Gonzalez, Sam Hauser, Paul George, and Neemias Queta when they face the Sacramento Kings.

That comes as the team is without Jayson Tatum, Mitchell Robinson, and Derrick White; Tatum is out to rest on the first night of a back-to-back, while Robinson is out with illness. White is out with a shoulder contusion that Mazzulla said he doesn't expect will be a long-term concern.

Mazzulla said he enjoys nights like Saturday, when the team will be shorthanded, and other guys will see more opportunities.

"Who's in, who's out — the standard and expectation stays the same," Mazzulla said. "You gotta be ready to play. Games like this, I enjoy because it validates the locker room and the roster, and guys work hard, put themselves in position to impact winning, and that's what we expect tonight."

Brad Stevens weighed in on the logjam at the wing position at shootaround on Saturday.

"I said at the start of the year, the biggest challenge for the coaching staff is the wing differentiation," Stevens said. "That's been the hardest part, and I think they've managed it beautifully, because nobody has really differentiated themselves, and they're all good players. And so that's just kind of the hard part about having depth, which in this league you need now. It's really important, but it also comes with the challenges of — not everybody's going to get exactly what they want on every given night. Is it easier to have seven or eight guys that you know are going to play every night? Yeah, until two of them get hurt, then you're in trouble, right? And so that's part of the challenge in this day and age with how the league is going and how hard it is to play the full 82, and certainly game to game with what's required on both ends."

Mazzulla said he didn't give players a heads-up on who will start or who will get more opportunities in the wake of the absences.

"We just need everybody to come in with the professionalism, the preparation that at any point in time they could [get] minutes to impact winning," he said. "So, guys do a great job of staying ready. It's on everybody to be prepared. We don't know what we're going to have to get to, so guys got to be ready. They do a good job of that, and we try to stay consistent in that."

#2859. I never wanted the American Dream and I felt like something was wrong with me—until I moved abroad

I got my first job at 17, working the register at an Eckerd's drugstore in Texas. My manager was only a few years older than me, and while my coworkers seemed motivated by the idea of working their way up to his position, I felt no desire to follow that path.

It all felt suffocating: the added responsibility, the longer hours, the idea that this was the ladder I was supposed to want to climb. I wanted to work, but I couldn't wrap my head around building my whole identity around a job.

I never wanted the American Dream I saw so many people around me striving to achieve. For a long time, I wondered if something was wrong with me. It wasn't until I finally made a long-term move abroad about a decade ago that I realized it was okay to want something different.

After college, I got a job in travel sales. I was good at it. Talking about travel all day suited the wanderlust I'd carried since my first trip abroad in my teens.

I continued working in sales, marketing and the odd temp role for years. But they were always just jobs. I didn't feel like they defined me or feel a calling to an industry or title the way people around me seemed to.

Of course, I wanted financial stability, but I was unwilling to sacrifice my time and well-being to get it. I just didn't connect with the version of ambition that meant grinding toward a corner office. What I wanted the most wasn't status; it was freedom, growth and new experiences.

The wanderlust eventually caught up to me, and at 26 I moved to Spain to teach English. Even though I was working, people saw it as taking a year off from my career and warned me it could damage my resume. I simply didn't care. Living for my resume had never made sense to me.

Looking back, I think being abroad offered an escape from the pressure to chase the traditional path of the American Dream. There, I felt free to enjoy life and figure out who I was without worrying about what box I needed to tick next.

I wanted to stay in Spain, but I couldn't find a visa that would let me live there long-term, so I returned to the U.S. I became a serial job hopper for almost 10 years, quitting a job every year to spend summers visiting friends in the Netherlands, the U.K. and Switzerland or solo traveling in Spain on the money I'd saved up.

On the personal side, I wasn't in a rush to get married or have kids. In my 30s, single and childfree, I felt like I was failing at adulthood, even though deep down, I didn't actually want the things I was supposedly behind on. Settling down just because everyone else was doing it never felt right to me.

And I couldn't shake the dream of living in Europe. In my 20s, people mostly assumed my desire to travel was a phase. By my 30s, the conversations had shifted and I was getting pulled aside. Friends, family and colleagues all had some version of the same message: It was time to get serious and build a life in Texas.

But my gut said to try one more time. So I did.

In 2015, on my fourth attempt, I finally made it to Spain for good. It was scary to shake things up when everyone around me was settling down, but deep down I knew at 35, this was my chance to stop trying to conform and start building a life that felt right for me.

In my new home, the pace was slower. I quickly noticed that people actually took their vacations. Strangers at a dinner party didn't immediately ask me what I did for work. Adults in their 30s, 40s and beyond were out living full social lives. Single, childfree women my age didn't feel like an anomaly.

Living abroad didn't solve everything overnight. I still had to figure out how to support myself. But having the space to define myself professionally without constant judgment changed what felt possible.

In 2017, I launched She Hit Refresh, where I help women over 30 figure out how to move to Europe and build new lives for themselves. It's not something I could have created from anywhere else, with any other experience than my own.

In the process, I've met so many others who left corporate America behind to create something different for themselves. I've seen more women asking the same questions I spent years asking myself. Burnt out, disillusioned with hustle culture, realizing that checking all the boxes of the American Dream they grew up on didn't deliver the feeling they were promised.

We're all figuring it out together; each of us writing a version of a life that didn't fit a standard template. Moving abroad helped me understand that success, for me, was never about earning more or owning more. It was always about more ease, more time, more space, more life.

For years, I thought I was behind. I felt like something was wrong with me for wanting a different kind of life. Looking back, I just hadn't found my place yet.

Cepee Tabibian is the founder of She Hit Refresh, where she's helped over 24,000 women age 30+ plan their move abroad. Grab her free Move Abroad After 30 Guide to start planning your own move. The daughter of Colombian and Iranian immigrants, Cepee grew up in Houston, Texas, before becoming an immigrant herself in Spain. Follow her @shehitrefresh.

#2857. The five players who have won the ATP Finals the most times ahead of Carlos Alcaraz vs Alexander Zverev

Carlos Alcaraz and Alexander Zverev will do battle on Sunday afternoon at the Inalpi Arena in Turin, Italy.

Zverev, the world number one, advanced to the ATP Finals championship match after defeating Daniil Medvedev on Saturday evening.

Earlier in the day, Alcaraz rallied from a set down against Ben Shelton to win 3-6, 6-4, 7-6.

Ahead of Sunday's blockbuster finale, let's take a look at the most successful individuals in ATP Finals history.

With seven titles to his name, Novak Djokovic has the best record of any player in ATP Finals history.

The Serbian superstar, who has also won 24 Grand Slam titles, won seven titles between 2008 and 2023, including four consecutive titles between 2012 and 2015.

Roger Federer is next on the list. The Swiss maestro won six titles before retiring in 2022 – from 2003 to 2004, 2006 to 2007 and 2010 to 2011.

Ivan Lendl and Pete Sampras land in third place. Sampras won five titles in 10 years between 1991 and 2000, while Lendl collected five crowns between 1981 and 1987.

Remarkably, Lendl qualified for nine consecutive ATP Finals championship matches between 1980 and 1988.

In fourth place is Romanian star Ilie Nastase. The two-time major singles champion won the title on four occasions, between 1971 and 1973, and in 1975.

John McEnroe and Boris Becker share the fifth spot with three titles to their names. McEnroe, who also won seven consecutive ATP Finals doubles titles alongside Peter Fleming, captured his singles titles at the event in 1978, and from 1983 to 1984.

Becker won his ATP Finals titles in 1988, 1992 and 1995.

Alexander Zverev, the world number one, will join Becker and McEnroe on three ATP Finals title wins if he defeats Carlos Alcaraz on Sunday.

#2856. Knicks' Mikal Bridges drills buzzer-beating 3-pointer to defeat Trail Blazers

The New York Knicks are rounding into form, and maybe Saturday night is what Mikal Bridges needed to get back on track, too.

The 30-year-old wing, who came into a matchup with the Portland Trail Blazers shooting just north of 40% from the field and a smidge above 30% from deep, drilled a buzzer-beating 3-pointer to deliver New York a 97-96 victory in Madison Square Garden.

Bridges finished with 12 points on 5-of-8 shooting. He connected on two of his three attempts from long range. The last one extended the Knicks' win streak to four games.

Following a pair of Deni Avdija free throws that put the Trail Blazers (10-6) up 96-94, New York (10-6) took a timeout. With 3.2 seconds remaining, Josh Hart inbounded the ball to Bridges.

He caught the pass to the right of the top of the arc, used a quick fake to get an overzealous Toumani Camara to bite, took one more dribble and launched.

The ball rattled in, the buzzer sounded and Mike Breen exclaimed, "Bang! Bang!"

Bridges was swarmed by his Knicks teammates, eventually wearing a smile as MSG reveled in utter pandemonium.

He came to the rescue on a night when New York squandered a 26-point lead. The Knicks shot 13 of 27 from 3-point land in the first half but scored only 31 points over the final two quarters.

Bridges admitted afterward that Portland played "harder" than New York in the second half.

But he also noted in that on-court postgame interview: "It's 48 minutes. We just kept persevering through the time and ended up winning the game."

After trailing 94-84 with less than five minutes to go, the Trail Blazers went on a 12-0 run, which culminated in the aforementioned Avdija free throws. Additionally, that stretch featured a game-tying 3-pointer from Damian Lillard.

Avdija was sent to the line because of a head-scratching foul by Knicks center Andre Drummond, who switched and committed the personal on the floor well beyond the arc.

Avdija had 20 points. Center Donovan Clingan spearheaded Portland's effort with 22 points and 13 rebounds. Lillard put up just 15 points and shot a meager 1 of 9 from outside.

New York was paced by Jalen Brunson's 30 points. Fellow guard Landry Shamet poured in 20 points.

Bridges, though, was the hero, sinking a redemptive shot 16 games into what's been a trying season for the ninth-year pro.

#2853. Will Bastian Assomo be an option at striker for Bayern Munich?

Bayern Munich is suddenly facing a significant challenge in its attacking options, with Harry Kane and Ismael Saibari both sidelined, but Vincent Kompany is not about to start throwing names at the wall and hoping something sticks.

With Bayern missing two of its primary attacking options, questions naturally turn toward who could step up, whether tactical adjustments are coming, and if younger players like Bastian Assomo could get an opportunity. Kompany, however, wants the club to assess its options before making any sweeping declarations about who will fill the void.

"We could name every player from our academy and youth teams, but let's first take a calm look at the situation, establish exactly where things stand with Michael and with the players we have available, rather than simply throwing out names in every direction. I'm not a fan of panic football. We need to stay calm, come up with a plan together and fight as hard as we possibly can. And then go out and get our results. As for who will play a role, we can't say that just yet. Every game will be a different story," Kompany said (as captured by @iMiaSanMia).

That last part is particularly telling. Bayern might not have one definitive replacement for Kane or Saibari, and Kompany appears prepared to approach the situation on a game-by-game basis rather than commit to a single solution prematurely.

The manager will need flexibility, creativity, and contributions from players who might not ordinarily be asked to shoulder this much responsibility. Bayern's depth will be tested, but Kompany's message is clear: there will be no panic, just a plan and an expectation to deliver results.

#2852. Mikal Bridges’ buzzer-beater saves Knicks from brutal choke

For most of this season, Mikal Bridges had been benched in these moments.

With the Knicks missing Karl-Anthony Towns (illness), OG Anunoby (left calf tightness) and Jordan Clarkson (right plantar fasciitis) on Saturday, Bridges found himself on the court in the closing moments, and the ball ended up in his hands with the game on the line.

Mikal Bridges hits the game-winning, buzzer-beating 3-pointer in the Knicks' 97-96 win over the Trail Blazers on Nov. 21, 2026, at Madison Square Garden. Getty Images

He promptly delivered what just might be his coming out party for this season, drilling a buzzer-beating 3-pointer as the shorthanded Knicks staved off the Trail Blazers 97-96 at Madison Square Garden to extend their winning streak to four games.

The Knicks saw a 23-point halftime lead disappear.

After Deni Avdija's free throws broke a tie to give the Blazers a two-point lead with 3.2 seconds left, the Knicks needed a hero.

A leaping Josh Hart celebrates with Mikal Bridges as Landry Shamet (left) looks to join in on the celebration after Bridges' buzzer-beating 3-pointer in the Knicks' win over the Trail Blazers at the Garden. Jason Szenes for New York Post

Bridges — amid a season of intense criticism and struggles — became one.

The play was, as expected, supposed to be for Jalen Brunson. But he was covered. So Bridges instead got the inbounds from Josh Hart, pump-faked Toumani Camara out of the way and drilled the game-winning 25-footer from the left wing.

"Knowing that any good and bad stretch in the NBA can change very quickly," Landry Shamet, one of Bridges' closest friends on the team, told The Post, "he's always in a position to put himself in a good situation."

With the game tied at 94-94 and the Knicks in the penalty, Andre Drummond committed a boneheaded foul on Avdija, who was dribbling in the opposite direction of the basket near halfcourt. Drummond later admitted he thought the Knicks had a foul to give.

Avdija's free throws looked like they would seal what would have been the Knicks' worst loss of the season.

Before long, though, the Garden was erupting, and Bridges' teammates surrounded him in celebration.

Jalen Brunson, who scored 30 points, celebrates after hitting a 3-pointer in the Knicks' buzzer-beating win over the Trail Blazers at the Garden. Jason Szenes for New York Post

"Shoutout to Mikal for making the big-time 3," Drummond said, "to kind of mask the nonsense that happened."

Nobody has come under more scrutiny than Bridges this season, and nobody needed a win more.

"That's just who he is," Brunson said. "I don't know how many times I've said this before, but when you continuously work on your game every single day, and things may not be falling in your favor, but you keep doing the right thing every single day, you keep chipping away, chipping away, eventually things are gonna turn for you. And over the past week or two, things have been turning for him."

Landry Shamet, who was in the starting lineup and scored 20 points, grabs a rebound during the first half of the Knicks' buzzer-beating win over the Trail Blazers at the Garden. Jason Szenes for New York Post

It was Bridges' second game-winner against the Blazers. He did it two years ago as well.

"I like the city of Portland," Bridges joked.

The Knicks are making a habit of seeing big leads dissipate. It hasn't come back to bite them yet, but they are treading in dangerous waters. Saturday's letup came on the heels of watching a 16-point fourth-quarter lead cut down to three points in the final moments of their win over the Timberwolves on Thursday.

Andre Drummond, who started in place of Karl-Anthony Towns (illness), looks to block Jayson Kent's layup attempt during the Knicks' buzzer-beating win over the Trail Blazers at the Garden. Jason Szenes for New York Post

They drilled their first eight 3-pointers and erupted for 40 points in the first quarter to jump out to a 17-point lead. Brunson, who made his first five 3-pointers, scored 19 points in that period and 23 by halftime. Shamet, continuing his red-hot start to the season, followed suit with 14 first-half points. The Knicks shot 53.2 percent from the field and 48.1 percent from deep in the first half and took a 23-point lead into the break.

They held Portland to a brutal 34.9 percent shooting from the field and 21.7 percent shooting from behind the arc in that first half.

Considering the quality of opponent and who the Knicks were missing, it was two of the best quarters the Knicks have played this season.

But the Knicks' red-hot shooting suddenly went ice-cold in the second half. They were outscored 29-15 in the third quarter and 24-16 in the fourth.

Their 31 second-half points were a dramatic drop-off from the 66 they erupted for in the first half. It was a complete role reversal, as the Blazers caught fire offensively — until Bridges bailed them out.

"He's been there and done that," head coach Mike Brown said. "So for him to have the ball in his hands at that point in the game, there's a lot of confidence not only coming from him but from his teammates and this staff."

The three absences meant a trio of usually end-of-bench reserves saw action. James Wiseman, Pacôme Dadiet and Dalton Knecht — who was called up from the G-League on Saturday — all featured in non-garbage-time minutes.

Dadiet actually was back on the floor to start the fourth quarter. In total, 11 Knicks saw the court — Mohamed Diawara was the only active player not to enter the game.

It meant a pretty uneven scoring output. Brunson and Shamet combined for 50, more than half of the team's points.

But it was Bridges who provided the most important ones.

#2851. Berkshire breaks out to record high with fourth-quarter rally reaching 19%

(This is the Warren Buffett Watch newsletter, news and analysis on all things Warren Buffett and Berkshire Hathaway. You can sign up here to receive it every Friday evening in your inbox.)

Berkshire Hathaway's red-hot shares are ending a strong week with four consecutive fresh record high closes.

Since the rally began with the start of the fourth quarter on October 1, the Class A shares have closed at record highs 11 times as they soared 19.1%.

The much more widely held B shares closed at new highs just 10 times but are also up 19.1% from their near-term low.

The gains are in sharp contrast to the S&P 500's 1.2% dip as investors appear to be seeing Berkshire as a relatively safe haven amid worries about AI stocks and spending.

Both Berkshire classes were up 3.9% on the week as the S&P advanced by 2.5%, its best week since early August.

Berkshire's B shares are now 7.5 percentage points ahead of the benchmark for the year so far.

After erasing their post-Buffett CEO announcement losses three weeks ago, Berkshire shares are now up almost 10% since last year's shareholders meeting.

They've gained around 29% since last August's lows and are up around 18% year-to-date.

Berkshire Hathaway is due to disclose its quarterly equity portfolio snapshot on Monday when it files its Q3 13F with the SEC.

We won't know for sure until then, but numbers in the company's third quarter 10-Q, released last Saturday, indicate Berkshire continued to reduce its Bank of America stake.

It would be the ninth consecutive quarter of selling.

As of June 30, Berkshire reported holding 483.4 million BAC shares valued now at $24.7 billion, it's fifth largest equity position.

On June 30, 2024, before the selling began, Berkshire owned just over 1 billion shares valued at $41.1 billion.

Since then, the number of shares in the portfolio has decreased by 53.2%, not including any Q3 sales, while the stock price has increased by 28.3%.

If there had been no reductions, the position would be worth $52.7 billion.

The belief Berkshire sold more BAC shares in the third quarter is based on a $372 million reduction in the cost basis for equities categorized by the company as "banks, insurance, and finance."

The 10-Q also reports $2.2 billion of pretax gains from equity sales.

Barron's notes Bank of America and American Express are the only financial stocks in the portfolio that have a cost basis low enough to generate a gain like that. Amex is one of the four stocks new CEO Greg Abel indicated would be held for "decades" to come in his February letter to shareholders.

In any case, Berkshire's almost 23% stake in Amex would have required it to quickly disclose any sales.

Based on the numbers in the 10-Q, the warrants Berkshire received as part of a 2011 $5 billion investment in the bank that it used in 2017 to buy 700 million shares at $7.14 each, and BAC's average stock price in the quarter, Barron's estimates 30 to 40 million shares may have been sold.

That would be roughly in line with the 30.2 million shares sold in the second quarter.

The case isn't as good, but Barron's thinks Berkshire may have bought more Alphabet shares in the period. That's based on a $2.3 billion increase in the cost basis for the category that includes Google's parent.

Overall, it looks like it will be a relatively quiet quarter with $2.4 billion in equity purchases and $2.8 billion in sales.

In the first quarter, Berkshire bought $15.9 billion and sold $24.1 billion of equities. In the second quarter, it purchased $23.5 billion and sold $3.7 billion.

All those numbers exclude the company's buybacks of its own stock.

In a term sheet filed early Friday, Berkshire Hathaway says it is selling more than 182 billion yen ($1.1 billion) worth of bonds with maturities between 2029 and 2056 and annual interest rates between 2.6% and 4.6%.

Bloomberg points out the rates, compared to benchmarks, are higher than its previous sale this year.

It quotes a fund manager as saying market sentiment "has become more sensitive," forcing issuers to make their offerings more attractive.

In April, Berkshire sold 272 billion yen ($1.7 billion) of bonds.

Berkshire uses the proceeds from the yen-denominated bonds to protect its ongoing investments in Japanese stocks from currency fluctuations.

Warren Buffett recommends Benjamin Graham's book, "The Intelligent Investor," and outlines what he considers Graham's three most important ideas.

WARREN BUFFETT: On investment books, if you're asking about that, I would recommend the first two books that Phil Fisher wrote back around 1960, "Common Sense [Stocks] and Uncommon Profits" and the second one ["Paths to Wealth Through Common Stocks"]. They're very good books.

You know, I obviously recommend, first and foremost, [Benjamin Graham's] "The Intelligent Investor," with chapters eight and 20 are the ones that you really should read.

Two of the — well, all of the important ideas in investing, really, are in that book, because there's only about three ideas. And those — two of them are emphasized in those two chapters...

One is that — to think of yourself — to think of investing as owning a business and not buying something that wiggles around in price.

And the second one is your attitude, which ties in with that, the attitude toward the market, that's covered in chapter eight. And if you have the proper attitude toward market movements, it's an enormous help in securities.

And the final chapter is on the margin of safety, which means, don't try and drive a 9,800-pound truck over a bridge that says it's, you know, "Capacity: 10,000 pounds." But go down the road a little bit and find one that says, "Capacity: 15,000 pounds."

Berkshire market capitalization: $1,259,897,054,755

Berkshire Cash as of Sept 30: $377.4 billion (Up 3.3% from June 30)

Excluding Rail Cash and Subtracting T-Bills Payable: $352.3 billion (Down 1.9% from June 30)

Berkshire repurchased $9.6 billion of its shares in Q3 2026.

Berkshire's top holdings of disclosed publicly traded stocks in the U.S. and Japan, by market value, based on the latest closing prices.

#2850. The bull market has a new problem. The 'smart money' is selling

Companies have shifted from buying back shares to issuing them, adding to the supply that the four-year-old bull market must absorb.

"No matter how you measure it, U.S. corporations have changed their behavior over the past two years, switching from repurchasing in 2024 to issuing today," Acadian Asset Management portfolio manager Owen Lamont wrote in a letter to investors this month. "The question you should be asking is: Why is the smart money selling?"

As of late last week, net new issuance of shares equaled 3.1% of total market capitalization, according to a recent Acadian analysis. While net issuance remains below the peaks during the tech bubble and the covid bubble, the analysis shows that the era of repurchases has ended and an era of equity issuance has begun.

And more supply came to the market this week with Anthropic's blockbuster IPO. The offering drew strong enthusiasm initially with the frontier AI laboratory selling shares in its IPO at $83 each, above the expected range and raising $81 billion. But the stock is falling on Friday and taking the broader stock market with it.

Some investors for years have cited the shrinking stock market as a reason why share prices can keep increasing. As companies repurchased stock, it shrunk the number of shares available for trading and so it helped boost prices. There was more money chasing fewer shares. Not to mention, it had the effect of boosting earnings per share by lowering the denominator in that ratio. Now that trend has reversed with increased supply acting as a potential headwind on the bull market.

A 2000 paper by Malcolm Baker and Jeffrey Wurgler found that when companies issued more equity over debt, low market returns followed, suggesting that managers timed the market when issuing securities. The Baker-Wurgler equity share, which measures equity issuance as a share of total stock and bond issuance, peaked at about 18% in 2021. It has risen in recent months but remains below that peak, Lamont wrote.

The Federal Reserve data show a similar trend in the year-over-year change in trailing 12-month gross stock and bond issuance. Debt gross issuance rose first in 2020, followed by equity issuance in 2021. More recently, debt issuance began climbing in 2024, followed by a surge in equity issuance in 2026.

Recent deals show companies tapping equity markets in different ways.

Oracle filed to sell $20 billion in stock through an at-the-market program and issued another $10 billion in common shares in November. In October, Meta priced a $25 billion common-stock offering and $12.5 billion in mandatory convertible preferred shares. Alphabet announced an $84.8 billion equity raise in June to help fund its AI infrastructure buildout. SpaceX raised $85.7 billion in its June IPO after underwriters exercised their overallotment.

"Before 2026, firms were net repurchasers of equity, consistent with the idea that they viewed their shares as undervalued. Today, for whatever reason, firms are issuing equity on net," Lamont wrote.

The investor said he cannot rule out that we are in an AI bubble now.

#2849. Brian Flores vs. Kyle Shanahan is ultimate 'Sunday Night Football' chess match | Opinion

Like you, me and millions of others, Phil Simms can't wait to see how it all goes down in Mexico City on Sunday night, when the NFL's most dominating defense collides with the league's top-ranked offense.

The Minnesota Vikings (7-2) have allowed just six touchdowns all season, yielding an NFL-low average of 14 points per game. The San Francisco 49ers (8-1) have scored more touchdowns (37) than any team in the league while amassing the most yards.

Something's gotta give. And oh, by the way: those big-time units just happen to be directed by two of the sharpest minds in the NFL universe, with Minnesota's D created by coordinator Brian Flores while 49ers coach Kyle Shanahan pushes the buttons for the prolific offense.

Complete with that matchup of wits, it's a subplot for "Sunday Night Football" (8:20 p.m. ET) that the folks at NBC are undoubtedly tinkled pink about.

"Without question, this is the best D-coordinator and the best O-coordinator in the NFL," Simms, the former Giants quarterback and Super Bowl XXI MVP, told USA TODAY Sports from his home in New Jersey. "If you try to argue that I would just go, 'You're stupid.'

"All I know isc in that chair, and I can't wait for this game."

If the names aren't moving enough – the 49ers surround Brock Purdy with Christian McCaffrey, George Kittle and Trent Williams, among others, while the Vikings bring Harrison Smith, Andrew Van Ginkel and Dallas Turner, among others – let a few more numbers speak.

The Vikings lead the league with 37 sacks and hold opposing QBs to the lowest efficiency rating at 69.5. The 49ers have given up the third-fewest sacks (13) while Purdy ranks second in the NFL with a 115.2 passer rating.

That efficient knack for Purdy will undoubtedly face its toughest test the season because the Vikings blitz at the highest rate in years (maybe ever), bringing extra rushers on more than 80% of the snaps. Purdy, in his fifth NFL season, can vouch for that. He was sacked six times and threw an interception in the last meeting between the teams, a 23-17 Vikings win in 2024. In 2023, Minnesota collected just one sack but intercepted Purdy twice in a 22-17 victory.

Shanahan said this week that experience has facilitated Purdy's growth to the point nothing will surprise him because there is little he hasn't seen. We'll see how that plays out. Flores' knack involves unpredictability that comes with designing blitzes that may not have shown up on video.

"Look, I played 15 years," Simms said. "I think I know every blitz, have seen it or whatever. But that's just not true. I watch the Vikings every week and it never fails. There's something new every week.

"I don't know if you can get ready for it in a week. That's one of their little secrets of power."

Still, as sharp as Flores is, there are other master strategists all across the NFL. Why is Minnesota's architect able to get away with such a blitz-heavy scheme – remember the tried-and-true football axiom, "live by the blitz, die by the blitz" – without getting burned?

While Shanahan mentioned the commitment to Minnesota's aggressiveness as vital to the effectiveness, listen to another Super Bowl MVP quarterback.

"They have so many smart players," Kurt Warner, who led the Rams to a Super Bowl 34 crown as triggerman for the "Greatest Show on Turf," told USA TODAY Sports.

"He does a great job teaching his guys the nuances, those details," Warner added of Flores. "It's, 'If we're going to do this, this is how it has to go.' But it's also having players with a great feel, great instincts. They understand the big picture. 'I've got to rush the C-gap, but I've got to know what the quarterback is doing and what the formation is.' They can add extra layers to it."

That includes the versatility that plays hand in hand with disguising intentions. Maybe no Vikings defender illustrates as much like Van Ginkel, the outside linebacker who has generated his team-high 8 ½ sacks on just 175 rush snaps, while also logging 103 snaps in coverage – more than any edge rusher in the league.

Of course, the 49ers offense has its own brand of versatility. McCaffrey, the NFL's leading scorer (80 points, including 13 TDs), has long been one of the league's most lethal dual threats with receiving skills out of the backfield on par with many wideouts. Deebo Samuel Sr. is the powerful receiver who frequently lines up in the backfield. Kyle Juszczyk is not your classic fullback, aligning all across the formation as another type of Swiss Army knife.

In some regards, including versatility, the Vikings defense and 49ers offense provide mirror images.

Which gets us back to Shanahan, long one of the league's best play-callers. As much as he marvels at play designs that create wide-open windows for passing targets, Simms notes the resourcefulness.

The 49ers have produced their big numbers this season – and tied for the most victories in the NFL – despite having the wide receiver corps decimated by injuries. Last year, Shanahan took the 49ers to the playoffs despite losing Purdy for several games on top of setbacks to Kittle and McCaffrey (in addition to the season-ending injuries to defensive stars Fred Warner and Nick Bosa).

"Kyle, he's going to do whatever it takes," Simms said.

He thought about the flow that Mac Jones had last season while filling in for Purdy and asked: "Look at Kyle Shanahan's career and tell me which quarterback has ever played bad under him?"

The lineup would include Kirk Cousins, back in Minnesota to revive the Vikings' playoff hopes. Matched against San Francisco's defense on Sunday night, he represents another layer of a subplot. Shanahan was Cousins' first NFL offensive coordinator when the quarterback broke into the NFL with Washington in 2012.

Here's to a great chess match. One thing for certain: There is no shortage of respect between Shanahan and Flores.

"Where do I start?" said Flores, using the same word – elite – to describe the 49ers' rushing attack, pass protection and play design. "All of it is elite."

This article originally appeared on USA TODAY: Brian Flores vs. Kyle Shanahan is ultimate 'Sunday Night Football' chess match | Opinion

#2847. Warren Buffett quadruples Thanksgiving gifts: A breakdown of how much wealth he's giving away

(This is the Warren Buffett Watch newsletter, news and analysis on all things Warren Buffett and Berkshire Hathaway. You can sign up here to receive it every Friday evening in your inbox.)

Warren Buffett, 96, more than quadrupled his Thanksgiving donations to four family foundations this year to $5.5 billion.

The gifts consist of Berkshire Class B shares that have been converted from his roughly $160 billion of Class A shares.

The increase is in line with his new goal, announced last July, to give away all of his Berkshire Hathaway shares "within about eight years," with no additional annual donations going to the Gates Foundation.

Around $2.2 billion of shares goes to the Susan Thompson Buffett Foundation, named for his late first wife, with the three foundations run by his children, Susie Buffett's Sherwood Foundation, the Howard G. Buffett Foundation, and Peter Buffett's NoVo Foundation each getting $1.1 billion each.

In July of last year, Buffett gave shares worth $4.6 billion to the Gates Foundation with another $1.4 billion going to the family foundations.

This July, the family foundations received $5.9 billion in stock, bringing the year's total to $11.4 billion, an increase of almost 55% from last year, even as the Gates Foundation received nothing.

In comments included in the Berkshire news release this week announcing the new gifts, Buffett reaffirmed his confidence in his children's ability to disburse his fortune over time, saying they "have given thoughtfully to worthy causes for decades, and I trust 100% both their abilities and their fidelity."

He also said his confidence in Berkshire is "as strong as ever."

Recalling that in last year's lengthier Thanksgiving message he had said, "Kindness is costless but also priceless," Buffett wrote, " A year later, I would only add that it compounds, and at a better rate than anything Charlie [Munger] and I ever bought."

"Both of us received beyond measurement costless benefits from a few teachers such as Ben Graham, Joe Rosenfield and Tom Murphy. Because of this we not only prospered mightily but were able to pass along many of the benefits we received.

"Much of my good fortune has come in the form of people who were kind to me and expected nothing in return. I'm grateful to every one of them.

"This Thanksgiving, thank one of those selfless people in your own life. Better yet, be a benefactor for someone else."

Berkshire Hathaway's fourth quarter rally that had produced a string of record highs cooled this week as both classes of its stock posted five straight daily declines.

Last Friday, Berkshire was up 19.1% since October 1. Now that gain has been cut to 14.5%

The S&P's decline of 8.6% this week, however, was even bigger than Berkshire's 5-day drop, as investors sold to lock in profits from AI-related stocks.

The benchmark index, along with the Dow Industrials, are in a correction after falling 10% from their recent highs, and the Nasdaq Composite has erased all of its year-to-date gains.

Berkshire's smaller decline increased its year-to-date outperformance of the S&P to 12.5 percentage points.

One Wall Street firm, however, thinks Berkshire's run-up has run its course.

Argus Research downgraded the B shares to hold from buy Friday, saying the stock is "fully valued" at about 25 times next year's estimate for operating earnings per share.

As foreshadowed by its 10-Q financial report released almost two weeks ago, Berkshire Hathaway's Q3 13F portfolio snapshot filed with the SEC on Monday showed it wasn't doing much buying and selling of equities this summer.

And the two largest additions, Lennar and Bending Spoons, had been previously disclosed, along with additional purchases at the start of the fourth quarter.

The most notable change we didn't expect was a 12% increase in Berkshire's Delta Air Lines position, bringing it just short of a 10% stake, now valued at $5.2 billion.

Exceeding 10% triggers a number of regulatory burdens, including the requirement to promptly disclose new buying or selling, so we may not see additional purchases going forward.

Delta's return to the portfolio after Warren Buffett sold all of Berkshire's airline stocks in 2020 has been something of a surprise given his bumpy ride with the industry over the years.

As expected, Berkshire continued to trim its Bank of America stake. After nine consecutive quarters of sales, it's been cut by 56% but remains the fifth largest portfolio holding at $21.4 billion.

Berkshire also cut another financial, Capital One, by almost 62%, although it was already a relatively small position, now worth $207 million.

Also notable are two positions that didn't change.

After adding $17 billion in Q2 to the Alphabet bet that had only been initiated last summer, Berkshire defied some expectations by keeping the holding unchanged.

Its 106 million shares are valued at $28.1 billion.

And for the fourth consecutive quarter, Berkshire's largest equity holding, Apple, didn't change. With a big boost from the stock's year-to-date gain of 26.1%, the position is now valued at more than $78 billion.

Warren Buffett loves to eat what many would call junk food and drink a lot of sugary beverages. A quarter-century ago, when he was 70, Buffett explained why he still thought a life insurance company would rate his life expectancy "considerably better than the average."

AUDIENCE MEMBER: Every time I hear what you like to eat, Warren, it makes me wonder what your cholesterol level is — (laughter) — or if you even worry about it...

WARREN BUFFETT: I do know the number, and I don't remember it. My doctor tells me, "It's a little high," but if he says it's a little high, it means it isn't that high, or he would — because he always tries to push me into making a few changes in my life.

But he — I've got a wonderful doctor. And I was lucky last year, because I hadn't been in to see him for about five years. And — (laughter) — due to — those guys cost a lot of money, I mean. (Laughter)

And due to purely an accident, a reaction to some other medicine I was given when I was out of the city, he got a hold of me, and then he shamed me into having a physical. And it was extremely lucky, because I had a polyp in the colon that would have probably caused trouble, you know, within a couple of years.

I would say that if you ask my doctor, he would want me to make a few changes, but he would also say that my life expectancy is probably a lot better than the average person of 70.

You know, I have no stress whatsoever. Zero. You know, I mean, I get to do what I love to do every day. You know, and I'm surrounded by people that are terrific.

So that problem in life just doesn't exist for me. You know, and I don't smoke or drink or, well, we'll end it right there. (Laughter)

And so, you know, if you were an underwriter for a life company, you would rate me considerably better than the average. You'd rate Charlie better than average, too.

And I'm sure that, you know, I could change it slightly, perhaps, on the probabilities, you know, if I change my diet dramatically or something. But it's very unlikely to happen.

Berkshire market capitalization: $1,210,976,168,580

Berkshire Cash as of Sept 30: $377.4 billion (Up 3.3% from June 30)

Excluding Rail Cash and Subtracting T-Bills Payable: $352.3 billion (Down 1.9% from June 30)

Berkshire repurchased $9.6 billion of its shares in Q3 2026.

#2846. San Antonio vs. New Orleans, Final Score: Wembanyama’s 50 points leads Spurs to 114-91 win

Victor Wembanyama blew up on the Pelicans with a 32 point first half showing and 50 overall, and while San Antonio (14-3) allowed New Orleans (5-13) make two runs in the first half, they were not troubled after the first half in winning 114-91. The Spurs outscored the visitors in the first three quarters, and Wembanyama and Stephon Castle slammed the door shut to get the starters some early rest before their brief West Coast road trip. Wembanyama (50 points, 19 rebounds, 5 blocks, and 4 steals) put up the types of numbers with this transcendent performance that might be used to make the case for him in the MVP battle with Luka Doncic.

Wembanyama (31 points on 12-for-15 FG, 13 rebounds, and 3 blocks) had the type of first half that so many bigs would kill for in a whole game. Stephon Castle (19 points and 6 rebounds) found his outside shooting touch with five threes. De'Aaron Fox dished out 10 assists.

Jeremiah Fears (29 points, 5 rebounds, 4 assists, and 2 steals) fearlessly kept the Pelicans in striking distance for 2+ periods. Jordan Poole (25 points) shot the ball well to support his backcourt mate as the pair made more than half of their team's field goals and points for the evening. Second year big Derik Queen chipped in 15 points and 15 rebounds.

With Zion Williamson (eye) being a late scratch minutes before tipoff, New Orleans looked out of sorts from the get-go. When the Spurs coasted their way to a 15-4 lead, Fears decided to take things into his own hands to nearly single-handedly erase that deficit in minutes. Poole came into the game and put up nine points frighteningly fast, and in a couple of blinks, the Pelicans were up three. Jordan McLaughlin helped preserve a disastrous quarter with hustle and grit, and San Antonio appeared lucky to be up 32-29.

Wembanyama asserted himself forcefully well at the start of the second period, as the Spurs went on a 13-2 run. His scores seemingly came from all over the court throuhgout the first half. The middle of the period run happened again as New Orleans threatened San Antonio with a 9-1 sequence. Fox, in particular, while kept out of the scorebook, was a noticeable menace on defense in guarding Fears. Wembanyama was the Spurs' fulcrum on offense (32 of the Spurs' 61) to help his team get out of the half up 16.

After Wembanyama's inevitable slowdown in the third period, Castle made the most of his diet of outside shots – with his four three-pointers putting New Orleans back on their heels and supplied San Antonio with the necessary cushion to secure its 14th win of the season.

I do like this version of Fox that's more pesky on both ends – he ran down Vassell's first miss, which ultimately led to a corner three and then he harangued Fears to cause a turnover on the ensuing inbounds. Fox, later in the half, bothered Fears into a backcourt violation and was whistled for a technical as the two had to be separated.

Though I'm not a native Spanish speaker, I thought it very humourous that a local mariachi band performed a song with "llorar" in its refrain after San Antonio ran its lead to 20.

Has there been a bigger dropoff from someone who was critical to his team's chances in an NBA Finals (2022) to wasting away in obscurity (2026) as Poole? There's also probably a similar, if not more statement to be made for Dejounte Murray… and well, Williamson whenever he changes teams.

The team that introduced the corner three as a useful offensive concept in the 2000s missed quite a bit of them this evening with an alarming amount of them airballs.

San Antonio and Memphis are the only undefeated teams left on the road(?!)

Fox-Slamma-Jamma-Wembanyama #1: While New Orleans pelted the rim and backboard with some unsightly attempts, Fox found his center with a lob partway through the opening stanza to push the Spurs lead to 11.

Carter Bryant really did seem confined to the Bruce Bowen corners for his offense in the first three quarters. He did connect on two threes later in the second half.

Sequence of the Game #1: Late in a wildly swinging first quarter, McLaughlin – giving 12 inches away to Karlo Matkovic, swiped the ball away from the big man, kicked it upcourt to Fox, who found Wembanyama for a non-shunk lob.

Sequence of the Game #2: Late in the third quarter, Wembanyama swatted away a Missi dunk attempt, and then drew an offensive foul call on Pierre, Jr. to cause the turnover.

Sequence of the Game #3: After Bryant missed a three in the fourth quarter, the long carom fell into Harrison Barnes' hands, and the second year guard cut baseline, received Barnes' pass, and threw down a thunderous two-hand slam.

Tarris Reed, Jr. (lithe AND bruising big man) playing time: In contesting a Poole floater, the rookie accidentally kneed the Pelican in the torso. He did get to four rebounds (two offensive) in his six minutes of playing tie.

Vassell bricked his first three attempts, but Wembanyama tip-slammed home the third try. Wembanyama, while passing it to Vassell, ended up smacking Queen right in the face, which took the Pelican momentarily out of the action. Two Champagnie triples put the Spurs up six. The Pelicans halfcourt offense during the initial minutes was really difficult to describe – Yves Missi managed to overshoot the rim by a good 5+ feet on a baseline jumper and many other possessions ended in long jumpers. Vassell willed in his fourth three attempt, and the San Antonio lead swelled to 11. Fears, like a robot that suddenly came to life, went on a 9-point burst, and with Poole's trio of threes, the Pelicans erased the deficit because… killer-instinct-still-in-process. Wembanyama then went on his own 9-point eruption to end the quarter with San Antonio up three.

San Antonio methodically started building out its lead to start the second. Then, on a foul committed on Wembanyama, someone named Jaron Pierre, Jr. shoved him after the play, and the rookie and Keldon Johnson were assessed double technicals. (It was cool to hear the referee refer to the superstar as 'Wemby' after the decision came back from Secaucus). The free throws pushed Wembanyama's point total past 20 a mere 14+ minutes into game action. The center's corner three then put the Pelicans down 12. However, right around the same time as it occurred in the previous period, New Orleans burned hot while the Spurs went cold, and a 14-point game became seven – with a Vassell free throw was the only Spurs damage during the Pelicans' 9-1 push. Then, just like in the first quarter, Wembanyama ran off a series of buckets to get himself over the 30-point mark, and San Antonio coasted into halftime up 61-45.

Normally one of New Orleans' biggest contributors, Bey's first and only points came two minutes into the third quarter. Castle, on a pair of catch-and-shoot threes, helped get the Spurs back up past 20. After San Antonio made it 69-48, Fears tried to kickstart yet another comeback attempt, but Castle countered with his third and fourth triples of the period preventing the Pelicans from trimming the deficit. Bryant snagged a pair of rebounds in traffic despite no offensive success to that point. Johnson and Fox took turns finding their superstar center deep in the paint for lay-up on successive possessions, and then teamed with each other for a quarter-ending Johnson three. The Spurs went to the fourth up 89-66.

#2845. Are the Rams playing today? NFL Week 11 schedule, next game, NFC West standings

It's been an up-and-down 2026 season for the Los Angeles Rams. Once the odds-on Super Bowl favorites, the Rams' outlook for the final half of their schedule is looking bleak after the devastating news that Puka Nacua's MCL surgery will leave him sidelined for the remainder of the regular season and the entirety of the playoffs.

And after general manager Les Snead only made marginal moves to address depth at the position at the trade deadline, reigning NFL MVP Matthew Stafford will now be throwing the ball to a position group led by Konata Mumpfield, CJ Daniels and Tutu Atwell.

The good news for the Rams is they won't have to pick up all the pieces on Sunday due to their bye in Week 11. Tight end Terrance Ferugson will miss Wednesday's Week 12 Thanksgiving eve matchup against the Green Bay Packers, but is expected to be activated off injured reserve before his 21-day practice window closes. Defensive lineman Kobie Turner, however, will be coming off IR and is expected to suit up against the Packers.

Despite four straight wins, the Rams' season outlook has dramatically plummeted over the last 48 hours.

Here's a look at their record, the NFC West standings and the Rams' remaining schedule as the season continues.

Week 3: Indianapolis Colts kicker Spencer Shrader (3) celebrates with long snapper Luke Rhodes (46) after Shrader kicked a 58-yard field goal against the Houston Texans at Lucas Oil Stadium. The Colts debuted their "Anvil Strike" uniform - featuring an unfamiliar gray uniform and glossy, metallic blue helmet with the familiar horseshoe logo in white. Week 3: Buffalo Bills quarterback Josh Allen (17) hands off to running back James Cook III against the Los Angeles Chargers at New Highmark Stadium. #2844. NCAA to scrap pregame handshakes at women’s tournament

The NCAA women's basketball tournament will no longer require head coaches to shake hands before games, according to a committee memo distributed to program administrators on Friday.

The memo notes that while the Division I women's basketball committee "encourages" coaches to take part in informal handshakes before team introductions, the gesture is no longer mandated.

The memo also outlined several operational changes regarding tournament media access. Media will no longer have locker room access, while pregame coach interviews at regional contests and the Final Four have been removed. Officials also eliminated required press conferences between the Sweet 16 and Elite Eight at regional locations.

Venues will also face new equipment standards in upcoming tournaments. For the 2027 opening round, along with the first and second rounds, shot clocks must display tenths of a second if the host venue has the technology. The feature is currently required at the Final Four and regional games, and it becomes mandatory for all rounds in 2028.

The NCAA men's and women's tournaments are expanding to 76 teams for 2027.

#2843. Victor Wembanyama says he was showing off for Selena Gomez while scoring 50 points in win over Pelicans

Victor Wembanyama outscored the New Orleans Pelicans' starters in the first half on Saturday night at home. He had 32 points. They had 26.

By game's end, Wembanyama had scored 50 points, his second 50-point performance of the season, this time in a 114-91 victory over the Pelicans (5-13) at Frost Bank Center.

The 7-foot-4 superstar went for 52 points in a Nov. 4 win over the Dallas Mavericks. Both of those outings have coincided with San Antonio (14-3) going 14-1 following an 0-2 start.

Wembanyama, still only 22, has already tied Spurs legend David Robinson for the second-most 50-plus-point games in franchise history with three. George Gervin, a Basketball Hall of Famer like Robinson, leads the way with five.

Wembanyama's got a long runway to surpass both Robinson and Gervin in that department. A third 50-piece this season would be historic in itself.

He's the third player since 1973-74 to log at least 50 points, 15 rebounds and 5 blocks in a single game. Wembanyama clocked out after shooting 20 of 29 with 18 rebounds, 5 blocks and 4 steals.

"It is very special," Wembanyama said, when asked about reaching the 50-point threshold in an on-court postgame interview.

He then said with a smile: "But unlike many of my peers, I'm not very good at stat padding. So I haven't reached it as many times as I should have."

Actress Selena Gomez was sitting courtside at the game. Wembanyama noticed.

"Of course, I had to show off for Selena, you know," he said, laughing.

The Spurs pulled away in the second quarter. San Antonio also got 19 points from guard Stephon Castle. Another backcourt contributor, De'Aaron Fox, piled up 10 assists.

The Pelicans are 1-9 in their last 10 games. They've come undone after beginning the 2026-27 campaign with a 4-4 record. A bright spot for New Orleans is the continued growth of 20-year-old guard Jeremiah Fears, who led the team with 29 points in the loss.

As for Wembanyama, he's once again an NBA MVP candidate. It's looking like he'll face stiff competition for that award this season as well.

Los Angeles Lakers star Luka Dončić has a pair of 50-point games under his belt, too, and he's leading the league in scoring with 37.2 points per contest.

#2842. This Anthropic proxy play is getting traders' attention – here's where they see it going

Who says you can't tailgate an IPO?Traders are preparing for this week's blockbuster Anthropic IPO in different ways, including buying options on one publicly-traded fund that invests in private assets.The Destiny Tech100 (DXYZ) is a publicly-traded, closed-end fund that invests in equity, equity-linked instruments, or shares of private funds and venture companies to gain exposure to private companies. Currently, 14% of the fund's assets are tied to Anthropic, according to the company's website.Options volume on the fund jumped to more than 10x the 30-day average Monday, according to Cboe LiveVol data. Of the 42,300 contracts traded by midday, 41,800 were calls, with just 141 puts likely opened by buyers.

Just shy of $5.5 million in options premium on DXYZ traded on Monday, with almost all of it tied to calls, but just marginally more spent buying than selling calls, according to SpotGamma. The highest-volume contract is currently the 30-strike call expiring Friday, a 90-cent contract that needs the fund to rally 19% into the weekend to pay off.Traders may want to be wary of how the fund traded around the SpaceX IPO: DXYZ surged almost 150% in May before unwinding all the gains in the weeks ahead of SpaceX's debut. Since then, the fund has barely moved, trading around $26.There are other bullish signs surrounding Anthropic's IPO in the derivatives markets. Current prices for perpetual futures on the blockchain exchange Hyperliquid are implying an opening price of $98, a big pop from the $70-$78 price range quoted in the company's amended S1 filing. The AI giant is expected to price Wednesday evening and begin trading on the Nasdaq on Thursday under the ticker ANT.
